Standing selector hypothesis. Whenever the analysis refers to a worst-case executed occupancy, we assume that for every Markov policy pair and stage h the inner infimum in the robust Bellman backup admits a measurable selector attaining the robust value. This makes the occupancy process $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ well defined. In finite / tabular settings this is automatic; more generally, one could replace attainment by an $\epsilon _ { \mathrm { s e l } }$ -optimal measurable selector at the cost of an additional $O ( \dot { H } K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { s e l } } )$ term in the final regret bounds.

Throughout the deterministic Bellman telescope, $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ denotes the stage-h occupancy of the executed policy pair under a worst-case selector that attains the robust value of that pair. For the statistical lemmas in the present on-policy core, we idealize the stage-h data batch as having state-action marginal $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ and next-state conditional law $P _ { h } ^ { 0 } .$ . Section A.11 later separates these two roles: it keeps $\widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k }$ for the worst-case executed occupancy used by the regret telescope, introduces a distinct datacollection occupancy for the empirical-process arguments, and adds an explicit transfer assumption between them.

For each episode k and stage h, define the exact robust residual and the operator error

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) : = f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) } ( s , a , b ) - ( \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) ( s , a , b ) , } \\ & { \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) : = f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) } ( s , a , b ) - ( \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) ( s , a , b ) , } \\ & { e _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) : = ( \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) ( s , a , b ) - ( \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) ( s , a , b ) , } \end{array}
$$

so that $\delta _ { h } ^ { k } = \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } + e _ { h } ^ { k }$ pointwise.

We will use the following three input conditions.

Assumption 5 (Approximate optimism). For every episode $k ,$

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } .
$$

Assumption 6 (Operator-learning budget). There exists a nonnegative array $\{ \alpha _ { h , k } \}$ such thatfor every k and $h ,$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| e _ { h } ^ { k } \right\| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \alpha _ { h , k } + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } , } \end{array}
$$

where $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ is the stage-h occupancy measure induced by the executed policy pair in episode k under a worst-case kernel selectorfor that pair.

Assumption 7 (Residual budget). There exists $\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) \geqslant 0$ such that

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) .
$$

Assumptions 6 and 7 are exactly the two places where the robust Markov-game extension of the statistical machinery enters. The approximate-optimism assumption is also not meant to be primitive: later in this appendix we show how it follows from retaining a suitable reference function inside the backward-validation confidence set.

## B.2 Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma 5 (Minimax value is 1-Lipschitz). Let M, $N \in \mathbb { R } ^ { | \mathcal { A } | \times | \mathcal { B } | }$ be two payoff matrices and define $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname { v a l } ( M ) : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { p \in \Delta ( A ) } \operatorname* { m i n } _ { q \in \Delta ( \bar { \mathcal { B } } ) } \bar { p ^ { \top } } M q } \end{array}$ . Then

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left| \operatorname { v a l } ( M ) - \operatorname { v a l } ( N ) \right| \leqslant \| M - N \| _ { \infty } . } \end{array}
$$

Proof. Fix arbitrary mixed strategies $p \in \Delta ( \mathcal { A } )$ and $q \in \Delta ( B )$ . Since $p , q$ are probability vectors,

$$
\left| p ^ { \mathsf { T } } ( M - N ) q \right| \leqslant \sum _ { a , b } p ( a ) q ( b ) \left| M _ { a b } - N _ { a b } \right| \leqslant \left\| M - N \right\| _ { \infty } .
$$

Therefore, for every $p ,$

$$
\operatorname* { m i n } _ { \boldsymbol q } \boldsymbol p ^ { \top } \boldsymbol M \boldsymbol q \leqslant \operatorname* { m i n } _ { \boldsymbol q } \boldsymbol p ^ { \top } \boldsymbol N \boldsymbol q + \| \boldsymbol M - \boldsymbol N \| _ { \infty } .
$$

Taking the maximum over p yields

$$
\mathrm { v a l } ( M ) \leqslant \mathrm { v a l } ( N ) + \| M - N \| _ { \infty } .
$$

Interchanging the roles of M and N gives the reverse inequality.

Lemma 6 (Approximate stage-game extraction). Suppose that for every $( h , s )$ , the extracted policy pair $( \pi _ { h } ^ { k } ( s ) , \nu _ { h } ^ { k } ( s ) )$ is $\epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } }$ -optimalfor the stage game induced by $f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) }$ , in the sense that

$$
V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , h } ( s ) \leqslant \mathbb { E } _ { a \sim \pi _ { h } ^ { k } ( \cdot | s ) , b \sim \nu _ { h } ^ { k } ( \cdot | s ) } \left[ f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) } ( s , a , b ) \right] + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } .\tag{11}
$$

Then the stage-game extraction error contributes at most $H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } }$ over one episode.

Proof. Equation (11) gives at most $\epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } }$ loss at each stage. Summing over the H stages of one episode gives the result. □

Lemma 7 (One-step robust Bellman comparison). Fix an episode k and abbreviate $f ~ = ~ f ^ { ( k ) }$ $\pi = \pi ^ { k }$ , and $\nu = \nu ^ { k }$ . Let $P _ { h } ^ { \dagger } ( \cdot \mid s , a , b )$ be a measurable selector that attains the robust value ofthe executed policy pair $( \pi , \nu )$ at stage h. Define

$$
\Delta _ { h } ( s ) : = V _ { f , h } ( s ) - V _ { h } ^ { \pi , \nu , \sigma } ( s ) .
$$

Then for every state s and stage h,

$$
\Delta _ { h } ( s ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \mathbb { E } \bigl [ \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ( s _ { h } , a _ { h } , b _ { h } ) + \Delta _ { h + 1 } ( s _ { h + 1 } ) \mid s _ { h } = s \bigr ] ,\tag{12}
$$

where the conditional expectation is taken under $a _ { h } \sim \pi _ { h } ( \cdot \mid s _ { h } ) , b _ { h } \sim \nu _ { h } ( \cdot \mid s _ { h } )$ , and $s _ { h + 1 } \sim$ $P _ { h } ^ { \dagger } ( \cdot \mid s _ { h } , a _ { h } , b _ { h } )$

Proof. By Lemma 6,

$$
V _ { f , h } ( s ) \leqslant \mathbb { E } _ { a , b } \left[ f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) \right] + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } .
$$

Subtracting the robust value of the executed policy gives

$$
\Delta _ { h } ( s ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \mathbb { E } _ { a , b } \left[ f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { \dagger } ( \cdot | s , a , b ) } \left[ V _ { h + 1 } ^ { \pi , \nu , \sigma } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] \right] .
$$

Add and subtract $\mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { \dagger } ( \cdot | s , a , b ) } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right]$ inside the bracket:

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \Delta _ { h } ( s ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \mathbb { E } _ { a , b } \left[ f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { \dag } } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] \right] } \\ & { \qquad + \mathbb { E } _ { a , b , s ^ { \prime } } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) - V _ { h + 1 } ^ { \pi , \nu , \sigma } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] . } \end{array}
$$

Because $\begin{array} { r } { ( { \mathcal T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ) ( s , a , b ) = r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) + \operatorname* { i n f } _ { P \in { \mathcal U } _ { h } ( s , a , b ) } \mathbb E _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] } \end{array}$ , we have

$$
r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) + \operatorname* { i n f } _ { P \in \mathcal { U } _ { h } ( s , a , b ) } \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] \leqslant r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) + \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { \dag } } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] .
$$

Hence

$$
\begin{array} { r } { f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { + } } \left[ V _ { f , h + 1 } ( s ^ { \prime } ) \right] \leqslant f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - ( \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ) ( s , a , b ) = \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) . } \end{array}
$$

Substituting this bound back proves (12).

Lemma 8 (Bellman telescoping inequality). Under the setting of Lemma $^ { 7 , }$

$$
V _ { f , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi , \nu , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ( s , a , b ) \right] + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } .
$$

Proof. Starting from (12) at $h = 1$ and taking expectations along the trajectory under the executed policy pair and the worst-case selector yields

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } \left[ \Delta _ { 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) \right] \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { 1 } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { 1 } ^ { k } \right] + \mathbb { E } \left[ \Delta _ { 2 } ( s _ { 2 } ) \right] . } \end{array}
$$

Iterating the same argument for $h = 2 , \ldots , H$ and using $\Delta _ { H + 1 } \equiv 0$ gives

$$
\mathbb { E } \left[ \Delta _ { 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) \right] \leqslant \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h } ^ { k } \right] + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } .
$$

Since $s _ { 1 }$ is deterministic, $\mathbb { E } [ \Delta _ { 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) ] = V _ { f , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi , \nu , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } )$

Lemma 9 (Residual and operator decomposition). For every episode k,

$$
\sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h } ^ { k } \right] \leqslant \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } \right] + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ | e _ { h } ^ { k } | \right] .
$$

Proof. The identity $\delta _ { h } ^ { k } = \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } + e _ { h } ^ { k }$ holds pointwise. Taking expectations under $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ and applying the triangle inequality to the operator-error term gives the claim. □

## B.3 Main Deterministic Reduction

Theorem 10 (Deterministic regret reduction). Under Assumptions $5 \mathrm { - } 7 ,$

$$
\mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) } + \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \alpha _ { h , k } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } .\tag{13}
$$

Proof. Fix an episode k. By Assumption 5,

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) .
$$

Applying Lemma 8 and then Lemma 9 yields

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } \right] + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left| e _ { h } ^ { k } \right| \right] .
$$

Summing over $k = 1 , \ldots , K$ gives

$$
\mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \sum _ { k , h } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } \right] + \sum _ { k , h } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ | e _ { h } ^ { k } | \right] .\tag{14}
$$

We now control the two sums in (14). By Cauchy–Schwarz,

$$
\sum _ { k , h } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } \right] \leqslant \sum _ { k , h } \sqrt { \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] } \leqslant \sqrt { K H \sum _ { k , h } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] } \leqslant \sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) } ,
$$

where the last step uses Assumption 7. For the operator error, Assumption 6 gives

$$
\sum _ { k , h } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left| e _ { h } ^ { k } \right| \right] \leqslant \sum _ { k , h } \bigl ( \alpha _ { h , k } + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } \bigr ) = \sum _ { k , h } \alpha _ { h , k } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } .
$$

Substituting both bounds into (14) proves (13).

We will also need a projected variant of the same reduction. Fix arbitrary stagewise maps $\mathsf { P } _ { h }$ $L _ { \infty } ( S \times \mathcal { A } \times \mathcal { B } ) \to \dot { L } _ { \infty } \overset { \vartriangle } ( S \times \mathcal { A } \times \mathcal { B } )$ . For each episode k and stage h, define the projected residual, projected plug-in residual, projected operator gap, and projection bias by

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } : = f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } , } \end{array}
$$

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } : = f _ { h } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { T } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ,
$$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { e _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } : = \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { T } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } , } \end{array}
$$

$$
b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } : = \mathsf { P } _ { h } \mathsf { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathsf { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } .
$$

Then $\delta _ { h } ^ { k } = \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } + b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k }$ and $\delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } = \widehat { \delta } _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } + e _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k }$ pointwise.

Theorem 11 (Deterministic regret reduction with projected completeness bias). Fix stagewise maps $\{ \mathsf { P } _ { h } \} _ { h = 1 } ^ { H }$ . Assume approximate optimism, and suppose there exists a residual budget $\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ( K ) \geqslant 0$ and a projection-bias constant $\epsilon _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } \geqslant 0$ such that

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left( \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ( K ) ,\tag{15}
$$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } \right\| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } \qquad f o r e \nu e r y ( h , k ) . } \end{array}\tag{16}
$$

Then

$$
\mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { P } } ( K ) } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } .\tag{17}
$$

Proof. Fix an episode k. By Assumption 5,

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) .
$$

Applying Lemma 8 gives

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h } ^ { k } \right] .
$$

Using the pointwise decomposition $\delta _ { h } ^ { k } = \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } + b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k }$ , we obtain

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \upsilon ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } \right] + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ | b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } | \right] .
$$

Summing over $k = 1 , \ldots , K$ and applying Cauchy–Schwarz to the projected residual term yields

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } \right] \leqslant \sqrt { K H \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left( \delta _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } \right) ^ { 2 } \right] } \leqslant \sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ( K ) }
$$

by (15). Equation (16) yields

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ | b _ { h , \mathsf { P } } ^ { k } | \right] \leqslant H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } .
$$

Combining the last two displays proves (17).

Corollary 12 (Main-text regret bound from statistical inputs). Suppose in addition that there exist absolute constants $C _ { 1 } , C _ { 2 } > 0$ such that

$$
\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) \leqslant C _ { 1 } H B _ { \phi } ^ { 2 } ( \sigma ) \ \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { B E , 2 } } ^ { \mathrm { r o b - M G } } ( \mathcal { F } , \Pi , 1 / \sqrt { K } ) \ \log ( K H / \delta ) ,
$$

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \alpha _ { h , k } \leqslant C _ { 2 } H B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { K } \log ( K H / \delta ) .
$$

Then Theorem 1 follows up to absolute constants and logarithmic factors.

Proof. Plug the two displayed bounds into Theorem 10. The residual term becomes

$$
\sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) } \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( H B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { B E } , 2 } ^ { \mathrm { r o b } \mathrm { - } \mathrm { M G } } ( \mathcal { F } , \Pi , 1 / \sqrt { K } ) K } \right) .
$$

The operator-learning term $\textstyle \sum _ { k , h } \alpha _ { h , k }$ is of order $\widetilde { \cal O } ( H B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { K } )$ , which is absorbed by the previous term because dim $\mathsf { \Pi } _ { \mathrm { B E , 2 } } ^ { \mathrm { r o b - M G } } ( \mathcal { F } , \Pi , 1 / \sqrt { K } ) \geqslant 1$ in any nontrivial problem. The remaining terms match the main-text statement. □

Iterate sample-complexity consequence. Under the assumptions of Corollary 12, there exists at least one iterate $k \in \ [ K ]$ such that

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) - V _ { 1 } ^ { \pi ^ { k } , \nu ^ { k } , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant \frac { \mathrm { R e g } ( K ) } { K } .
$$

Consequently, $\mathrm { i f } \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + H \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } }$ is at most order ϵ, then an iterate whose one-sided comparator difference is at most order ϵ is guaranteed once

$$
K = \tilde { \cal O } \left( \frac { H ^ { 2 } B _ { \phi } ^ { 2 } ( \sigma ) \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { B E , 2 } } ^ { \mathrm { r o b - M G } } ( \mathcal { F } , \Pi , 1 / \sqrt { K } ) } { \epsilon } \right) .
$$

If every iterate had comparator difference strictly larger than $\mathrm { R e g } ( K ) / K$ , then summing over all K iterates would contradict the definition of regret; the displayed sample-complexity condition is obtained by requiring each term in Theorem 1 divided by $\dot { K }$ to be at most order ϵ.

## C Residual Complexity and Statistical Verification

## C.1 Finite-State Fail-State Support-Control Argument

We now prove a concrete finite-state support-preservation statement. The proof only uses the failurestate geometry and does not depend on Bellman–Eluder arguments.

Proposition 13 (Support-preserving optimizer under TV balls). Assume a finite state space and fix a tuple $( h , s , a , b )$ . Let $P ^ { 0 } \in \Delta ( { \mathcal { S } } )$ be the nominal next-state distribution and let $s _ { F } \in \bar { \mathrm { s u p p } } ( P ^ { 0 } )$ be a failure state satisfying $\begin{array} { r } { V ( s _ { F } ) = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { x \in { \cal S } } V ( x ) = 0 . } \end{array}$ . Consider the optimization problem

$$
\operatorname* { i n f } _ { P \in \Delta ( \mathcal { S } ) : D _ { \mathrm { T V } } ( P , P ^ { 0 } ) \leqslant \sigma } \mathbb { E } _ { x \sim P } \left[ V ( x ) \right] .
$$

Then there exists an optimal solution $P ^ { \star }$ such that $\operatorname { s u p p } ( P ^ { \star } ) \subseteq \operatorname { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } )$

Proof. Let $Q$ be any feasible distribution. If sup $\rho ( Q ) \subseteq { \mathrm { s u p p } } ( P ^ { 0 } )$ , there is nothing to prove. Otherwise define the outside-support mass

$$
m : = \sum _ { x \notin \mathrm { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } ) } Q ( x ) > 0 .
$$

Construct a new distribution $\widetilde { Q }$ by moving all outside-support mass to the failure state:

$$
\widetilde Q ( x ) : = \left\{ \begin{array} { l l } { 0 , } & { x \notin \mathrm { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } ) , } \\ { Q ( s _ { F } ) + m , } & { x = s _ { F } , } \\ { Q ( x ) , } & { \mathrm { o t h e r w i s e } . } \end{array} \right.
$$

Clearly $\widetilde Q \in \Delta ( S )$ and $\operatorname { s u p p } ( \widetilde { Q } ) \subseteq \operatorname { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } )$

We first show that the objective does not increase. Since $V ( s _ { F } ) = 0$ and $V ( x ) \geqslant 0$ for all x,

$$
\begin{array} { l } { \displaystyle \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { Q } } \left[ V \right] = \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } ) } \widetilde { Q } ( x ) V ( x ) = \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } ) } Q ( x ) V ( x ) + m V ( s _ { F } ) } \\ { \displaystyle = \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } ) } Q ( x ) V ( x ) \leqslant \sum _ { x \in S } Q ( x ) V ( x ) = \mathbb { E } _ { Q } \left[ V \right] . } \end{array}
$$

We next show that the TV distance does not increase. Since $P ^ { 0 } ( x ) = 0$ for x $\notin \operatorname { s u p p } ( P ^ { 0 } )$ ,

$$
\begin{array} { l } { | | \vec { Q } - P ^ { 0 } | | _ { 1 } = | \vec { Q } ( s _ { F } ) - P ^ { 0 } ( s _ { F } ) | + \displaystyle \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) } | Q ( x ) - P ^ { 0 } ( x ) | } \\ { = | Q ( s _ { F } ) + m - P ^ { 0 } ( s _ { F } ) | + \displaystyle \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) } | Q ( x ) - P ^ { 0 } ( x ) | } \\ { \leqslant | Q ( s _ { F } ) - P ^ { 0 } ( s _ { F } ) | + m + \displaystyle \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) } | Q ( x ) - P ^ { 0 } ( x ) | } \\ { = \displaystyle \sum _ { x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) \atop x \in \mathrm { s u p p } ( I ^ { 0 } ) } | Q ( x ) - P ^ { 0 } ( x ) | } \\ { = | Q ( x ) - P ^ { 0 } | | . } \end{array}
$$

Therefore $D _ { \mathrm { T V } } ( \widetilde Q , P ^ { 0 } ) \leqslant D _ { \mathrm { T V } } ( Q , P ^ { 0 } ) \leqslant \sigma$ , so $\widetilde { Q }$ is feasible and has no larger objective value. Starting from an optimal $Q$ and applying this transformation yields an optimal solution supported inside supp $( P ^ { 0 } )$ . □

Corollary 14. Under the assumptions of Proposition 13, the robust Bellman target for the failure-state normalized valuefunction can be realized without leaving the nominal support.

Proof. Apply Proposition 13 at each state-action tuple to the continuation value $V = V _ { f , h + 1 }$ . The proposition yields an optimal robust kernel whose support is contained in that of the nominal kernel. Hence the robust Bellman target remains an in-support expectation. □

## C.2 A Robust MG-BE Counting Lemma

We now formulate a stage-wise counting lemma that captures the exploration term. Because the main text only uses an informal notion of distributional Eluder dimension, we state here a square-loss version tailored to the residual analysis.

Definition 15 (Square-loss distributional independence). Let $\mathcal { P }$ be a class ofreal-valuedfunctions on $S \times A \times B$ , and let $\Pi _ { h }$ be afamily ofprobability measures on the same domain. For $\epsilon > 0$ , we say that a measure $\mu \in \Pi _ { h }$ is ϵ-dependent on $\{ \mu ^ { 1 } , \ldots , \mu ^ { m } \} \subseteq \Pi _ { h }$ with respect to $\mathcal { P } i f$

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \mu } \left[ \psi ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \epsilon ^ { 2 } + \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { m } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu ^ { i } } \left[ \psi ^ { 2 } \right] \qquad f o r \ : a l l \ : \psi \in \mathcal { P } .
$$

Otherwise, µ is ϵ-independent. The square-loss distributional Eluder dimension, denoted by d $\mathrm { l i m } _ { \mathrm { D E } , 2 } ( \mathcal { P } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon )$ , is the length of the longest sequence in $\Pi _ { h }$ whose every element is ϵ-independent ofits predecessors.

Definition 16 (Square-loss robust MG-BE dimension). For the exact robust residual class $\mathcal { P } _ { h } : =$ $( I - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } ) \mathcal { F }$ , define

$$
\mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { B E , 2 } } ^ { \mathrm { r o b - M G } } ( \mathcal { F } , \Pi , \epsilon ) : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { D E } , 2 } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon ) .
$$

Lemma 17 (Robust MG-BE counting lemma). Fix a stage h and let $\mathcal { P } _ { h } = ( I - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } ) \mathcal { F } .$ . Suppose we are given a sequence $\{ ( \mu _ { h } ^ { k } , \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) \} _ { k = 1 } ^ { K }$ such that $\mu _ { h } ^ { k } \in \Pi _ { h } , \psi _ { h } ^ { k } \in \mathcal P _ { h }$ , and for every k,

$$
\sum _ { j < k } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \epsilon ^ { 2 } ,\tag{18}
$$

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] > 2 \epsilon ^ { 2 } .\tag{19}
$$

Then

$$
\begin{array} { r } { K \leqslant \dim _ { \mathrm { D E } , 2 } \big ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon \big ) . } \end{array}
$$

Proof. Fix $k \in [ K ]$ . By (18) and (19),

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] > \epsilon ^ { 2 } + \sum _ { j < k } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] .
$$

Hence the witness $\psi _ { h } ^ { k } \in \mathcal { P } _ { h }$ certifies that $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ is ϵ-independent of $\{ \mu _ { h } ^ { 1 } , \ldots , \mu _ { h } ^ { k - 1 } \}$ in the sense of Definition 15. Since this holds for every k, the sequence $\{ \mu _ { h } ^ { 1 } , \ldots , \mu _ { h } ^ { K } \}$ } is an ϵ-independent sequence in $\Pi _ { h }$ . By the definition of dim $\mathbf { \sigma } _ { \mathrm { E , 2 } } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon )$ , its length cannot exceed that dimension. □

Corollary 18 (Residual-budget corollary under backward validation). Fix a stage h and suppose there exists $B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h } < \infty$ such that $| \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ( \bar { s } , a , b ) | \leqslant B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h }$ for all $( s , a , b )$ , all k, and all $\psi _ { h } ^ { k } \in { \mathcal P } _ { h }$ Assume moreover that the sequence $\{ ( \bar { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } , \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) \} _ { k = 1 } ^ { K }$ satisfies the backward-validation condition (18). Then

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant 2 K \epsilon ^ { 2 } + \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { D E } , 2 } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon ) B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h } ^ { 2 } .
$$

Consequently,

$$
\sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant 2 H K \epsilon ^ { 2 } + \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { D E } , 2 } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon ) B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h } ^ { 2 } .
$$

Proof. For a fixed stage h, split the episode indices into

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathcal { L } _ { h } : = \left\{ k \in \left[ K \right] : \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] > 2 \epsilon ^ { 2 } \right\} , } \\ & { \mathcal { S } _ { h } : = [ K ] \setminus \mathcal { L } _ { h } . } \end{array}
$$

By Lemma 17, we have $| { \mathcal { L } } _ { h } | \leqslant$ di $\mathrm { m } _ { \mathrm { D E , 2 } } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon )$ . Therefore,

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } {  { \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } [ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } ] = \sum _ { k \in S _ { h } } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } [ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } ] + \sum _ { k \in \mathcal { L } _ { h } } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } [ ( \psi _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } ] } } \\ & { } & { \leqslant 2 K \epsilon ^ { 2 } + | \mathcal { L } _ { h } | B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h } ^ { 2 } } \\ & { } & { \leqslant 2 K \epsilon ^ { 2 } + \mathrm { d i m } _ { \mathrm { D E , 2 } } ( \mathcal { P } _ { h } , \Pi _ { h } , \epsilon ) B _ { \mathrm { r e s } , h } ^ { 2 } . } \end{array}
$$

Summing this inequality over $h \in [ H ]$ gives the second claim.

Corollary 18 reduces the residual-budget input Assumption 7 to a backward-validation condition on the exact robust residual witnesses. We now verify the two bridge terms (21) and (27) directly for the current backward-validation update.

## C.3 Verifying the Backward-Validation Bridge for the Current Update

For a fixed stage $h ,$ define the plug-in residual witness class

$$
\mathcal { W } _ { h } : =  \begin{array} { c } { f \in \mathcal { F } , \quad g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } , } \\ { w _ { h , f , g } : w _ { h , f , g } ( s , a , b ) = f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) } \\ { + \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } [ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) ]  . } \end{array} 
$$

By construction, for every $k \geqslant 2$ and every $f \in { \mathcal { F } } _ { \mathrm { ~  ~ } }$

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ) = w _ { h , f , g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } } \in \mathcal { W } _ { h } .
$$

Assumption 8 (Bounded plug-in residual witnesses). For thefixed stage h, there exists $B _ { W , h } < \infty$ such that

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { h } } \| w \| _ { \infty } \leqslant B _ { W , h } .
$$

Assumption 9 (Finite witness covering number). For the chosen witness scale η<sub>W</sub> $> 0 _ { ; }$

$$
\mathcal { N } _ { W } ( \eta _ { W } ) : = \mathcal { N } \big ( \mathcal { W } _ { h } , \| \cdot \| _ { \infty } , \eta _ { W } \big ) < \infty .
$$

Proposition 19 (Witness covering from stagewise value and dual covers). For a fixed stage $h ,$ define the stagewise sup-norm covering number

$$
\mathcal { N } _ { F , h } ^ { \infty } ( \eta _ { h } ) : = \mathcal { N } \big ( \mathcal { F } _ { h } , \| \cdot \| _ { \infty } , \eta _ { h } \big ) .
$$

Assume $\mathcal { N } _ { F , h } ^ { \infty } ( \eta _ { h } ) < \infty$ and Assumptions 11 and 12 hold. Then

$$
\mathcal { N } ( \mathcal { W } _ { h } , \| \cdot \| _ { \infty } , \eta _ { h } + L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) ) \leqslant \mathcal { N } _ { F , h } ^ { \infty } ( \eta _ { h } ) \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F } ) \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } ) .\tag{20}
$$

In particular, Assumption 9follows once the stagewise value slice $\mathcal { F } _ { h } ,$ , the continuation slice $\mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and the dual class $\bar { \mathcal { G } _ { h } }$ all admitfinite covers.

Proof. Let $\{ u ^ { r } \} _ { r = 1 } ^ { M _ { h } } \subseteq { \mathcal F } _ { h }$ be an $\eta _ { h }$ -net under $\| \cdot \| _ { \infty } ,$ let $\{ f ^ { m } \} _ { m = 1 } ^ { M _ { F } } \subseteq { \mathcal { F } } _ { h + 1 }$ be an η<sub>F</sub>-net under $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 }$ , and let $\{ g ^ { \ell } \} _ { \ell = 1 } ^ { M _ { G } } \subseteq \mathcal G _ { h }$ be an η<sub>G</sub>-net under $d _ { \mathcal { G } , h }$

Fix any witness $w _ { h , f , g } \in \mathcal { W } _ { h }$ . Choose $\bar { u } \in \{ u ^ { r } \} _ { r = 1 } ^ { M _ { h } } , \bar { f } \in \{ f ^ { m } \} _ { m = 1 } ^ { M _ { F } }$ , and $\bar { g } \in \{ g ^ { \ell } \} _ { \ell = 1 } ^ { M _ { G } }$ such that

$$
\| f _ { h } - \bar { u } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant \eta _ { h } , \qquad d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } , \bar { f } ) \leqslant \eta _ { F } , \qquad d _ { \mathcal { G } , h } ( g , \bar { g } ) \leqslant \eta _ { G } .
$$

Define

$$
\bar { w } ( s , a , b ) : = \bar { u } ( s , a , b ) - r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) + \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( \bar { f } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \bar { g } ) \right] .
$$

By the stagewise product interpretation $\mathcal { F } = \{ \mathcal { F } _ { t } \} _ { t = 1 } ^ { H }$ from the preliminaries, the pair $( \bar { u } , \bar { f } )$ can be completed to a candidate function sequence in $\mathcal { F }$ , so w¯ $\in \mathcal { W } _ { h }$

For every $( s , a , b )$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \left| w _ { h , f , g } ( s , a , b ) - \bar { w } ( s , a , b ) \right| \leqslant \left| f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \bar { u } ( s , a , b ) \right| } \\ & { \qquad + \left| \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) - \ell _ { \phi } ( \bar { f } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \bar { g } ) \right] \right| } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant \eta _ { h } + L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) , } \end{array}
$$

where the last step uses Assumption 11 and Jensen’s inequality. Hence the set of all such w¯ forms an $\eta _ { h } + L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } )$ )-net for $\mathcal { W } _ { h }$ of size at most $M _ { h } M _ { F } M _ { G }$ , which proves (20). □

For $k \geqslant 2 .$ , write

$$
n _ { h , j } : = | \mathcal { D } _ { h } ^ { ( j ) } | , \qquad S _ { h , k } : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } , \qquad N _ { h , < k } : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } n _ { h , j } , \qquad n _ { h , \mathrm { m i n } } ^ { ( k ) } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { 1 \leqslant j \leqslant k - 1 } n _ { h , j } .
$$

We also keep the shorthand

$$
\operatorname { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \operatorname { p l u g } } ( f ) : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] ,
$$

$$
\operatorname { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { e x a c t } } ( f ) : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \delta _ { h } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] ,
$$

$$
\widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ( f ) : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } \sum _ { ( s , a , b , r , s ^ { \prime } ) \in \mathcal { D } _ { h } ^ { ( j ) } } \left[ \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ; s , a , b ) \right] ^ { 2 } .
$$

Lemma 20 (Uniform backward-validation deviation). Under Assumptions 8 and 9, for every $k \geqslant 2$ and every $\delta \in ( 0 , 1 )$ , with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$

$$
\underset { f \in \mathcal { F } } { \operatorname* { s u p } } \Bigl | \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ( f ) - \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ) \Bigr | \leqslant \xi _ { h , k } \bigl ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } \bigr ) ,\tag{21}
$$

where

$$
\xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) : = B _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { 2 S _ { h , k } \log ( 2 \mathcal { N } _ { W } ( \eta _ { W } ) / \delta ) + 2 B _ { W , h } ( k - 1 ) \eta _ { W } } .
$$

Proof. Let $\{ w ^ { 1 } , \ldots , w ^ { M } \} \subseteq \mathcal { W } _ { h }$ be an η -net under the sup norm, where $M = \mathcal { N } _ { W } ( \eta _ { W } )$ . Fix one net witness $w ^ { m }$ . For every batch $j < k$ and sample index $i \in [ n _ { h , j } ]$ , define

$$
Y _ { j , i } ^ { ( m ) } : = \frac { \left( w ^ { m } ( s _ { j , i } , a _ { j , i } , b _ { j , i } ) \right) ^ { 2 } - \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( w ^ { m } ) ^ { 2 } \right] } { n _ { h , j } } .
$$

Conditioned on the history before the $( j , i )$ -th stage-h sample is revealed, $Y _ { j , i } ^ { ( m ) }$ has conditional mean zero, because that sample is drawn from the stage-h occupancy law $\mu _ { h } ^ { j }$ . By Assumption 8,

$$
\big | Y _ { j , i } ^ { ( m ) } \big | \leqslant \frac { B _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } } { n _ { h , j } } .
$$

Hence Azuma–Hoeffding for bounded martingale differences implies

$$
\mathbb { P } \left( \left| \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n _ { h , j } } Y _ { j , i } ^ { ( m ) } \right| > t \right) \leqslant 2 \exp \left( - \frac { t ^ { 2 } } { 2 B _ { W , h } ^ { 4 } S _ { h , k } } \right) .
$$

Applying a union bound over the M net witnesses gives that with probability at least $1 - \delta$

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { m \in [ M ] } \left. \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n _ { h , j } } ( w ^ { m } ( s _ { j , i } , a _ { j , i } , b _ { j , i } ) ) ^ { 2 } - \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( w ^ { m } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \right. \leqslant B _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { 2 S _ { h , k } \log \left( 2 M / \delta \right) } .\tag{22}
$$

Now fix an arbitrary witness $w \in \mathcal { W } _ { h }$ , and choose a net point w¯ such that $\| w - \bar { w } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant \eta _ { W }$ . Since $| w + \bar { w } | \leqslant 2 B _ { W , h }$ , we have

$$
\| w ^ { 2 } - \bar { w } ^ { 2 } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant \| w - \bar { w } \| _ { \infty } \| w + \bar { w } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant 2 B _ { W , h } \eta _ { W } .
$$

Therefore

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \displaystyle \left| \displaystyle \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n _ { h , j } } w ( s _ { j , i } , a _ { j , i } , b _ { j , i } ) ^ { 2 } - \displaystyle \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n _ { h , j } } \bar { w } ( s _ { j , i } , a _ { j , i } , b _ { j , i } ) ^ { 2 } \right| \leqslant 2 B _ { W , h } ( k - 1 ) \eta _ { W } , } \\ { \displaystyle \left| \displaystyle \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ w ^ { 2 } \right] - \displaystyle \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \bar { w } ^ { 2 } \right] \right| \leqslant 2 B _ { W , h } ( k - 1 ) \eta _ { W } . } \end{array}
$$

Combining these two inequalities with (22) yields

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { h } } \left. \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { 1 } { n _ { h , j } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n _ { h , j } } w ( s _ { j , i } , a _ { j , i } , b _ { j , i } ) ^ { 2 } - \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ w ^ { 2 } \right] \right. \leqslant \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) .
$$

Since every selected witness $\widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f )$ belongs to $\mathcal { W } _ { h }$ , this proves (21).

We next introduce the dual setup shared by the pooled backward-validation analysis and the on-policy operator-learning analysis. For a generic probability measure ν on $S \times A \times \dot { B _ { \cdot } }$ , define

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \nu } ( g ; f ) : = \mathbb { E } _ { ( s , a , b ) \sim \nu } \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) \right] .
$$

We also define an ambient dual class $\bar { \mathcal { G } } _ { h }$ large enough that the exact robust Bellman operator admits the pointwise representation

$$
( \mathcal T _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ) ( s , a , b ) = r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { \bar { g } \in \mathcal { \bar { G } } _ { h } } \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \bar { g } ) \right] .\tag{23}
$$

Assumption 10 (Bounded dual loss). For thefixed stage $h ,$

$$
\big | \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) \big | \leqslant B _ { \phi } ( \sigma )
$$

for every $( f , g ) \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } \times \mathcal { G } _ { h }$ and every sample $( s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } )$

Assumption 11 (Pointwise Lipschitz dual loss). There exist metrics $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 }$ on $\mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ <sub>1</sub> and $d _ { \mathcal { G } , h }$ on $\mathcal { G } _ { h } .$ , together with a constant $L _ { \ell , h } \geqslant 0 ,$ , such thatfor every $f , \tilde { f } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } , g , \tilde { g } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } ,$ , and every sample $( s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } )$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \bigl | \ell _ { \phi } \bigl ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g \bigr ) - \ell _ { \phi } \bigl ( \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \tilde { g } \bigr ) \bigr | \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } \Bigl ( d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } \bigl ( f , \tilde { f } \bigr ) + d _ { \mathcal { G } , h } \bigl ( g , \tilde { g } \bigr ) \Bigr ) . } \end{array}
$$

Assumption 12 (Finite covering numbers at the working scales). For the chosen scales $\eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } > 0$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F } ) : = \mathcal { N } \big ( \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } , d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } , \eta _ { F } \big ) < \infty , } \\ & { \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } ) : = \mathcal { N } \big ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } , d _ { \mathcal { G } , h } , \eta _ { G } \big ) < \infty . } \end{array}
$$

Assumption 13 (Dual approximation error). For every $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and every probability measure ν on $S \times { \bar { \mathcal { A } } } \times B ,$

$$
\operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \nu } ( g ; f ) \leqslant \operatorname* { i n f } _ { \bar { g } \in \bar { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \nu } ( \bar { g } ; f ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } .
$$

We next close the operator-transfer term generated by the same pooled dual refit used by the algorithm. Define the weighted past-occupancy mixture

$$
\bar { \mu } _ { h , k } : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \lambda _ { h , j } ^ { ( k ) } \mu _ { h } ^ { j } , \qquad \lambda _ { h , j } ^ { ( k ) } : = \frac { n _ { h , j } } { N _ { h , < k } } .
$$

For any $( f , g ) \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } \times \mathcal { G } _ { h }$ , define the pooled population dual loss

$$
\begin{array} { l } { \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) : = \displaystyle \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \lambda _ { h , j } ^ { ( k ) } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } ( g ; f ) } \\ { \displaystyle \qquad = \mathbb { E } _ { ( s , a , b ) \sim \bar { \mu } _ { h , k } } \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) \right] . } \end{array}
$$

The corresponding empirical pooled loss is

$$
\widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ; f ) : = \frac { 1 } { N _ { h , < k } } \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \sum _ { ( s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ) \in \mathcal { D } _ { h } ^ { ( j ) } } \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) .
$$

Let $g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } \in \mathrm { a r g m i n } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ; f )$ denote the pooled dual fit used by the current algorithm.

Lemma 21 (Pooled dual concentration for the current update). Under Assumptions $\ k l O { - } l 2 ,$ , for every $k \geqslant 2$ and every $\delta \in ( 0 , 1 )$ , with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { f \in \mathscr { F } _ { h + 1 } , g \in \mathscr { G } _ { h } } \left| \widehat { \mathscr { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ; f ) - \bar { \mathscr { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) \right| \leqslant \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) ,\tag{24}
$$

where

$$
\bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) : = B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \frac { 2 \log \left( 2 \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F } ) \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } ) / \delta \right) } { N _ { h , < k } } } + 2 L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) .
$$

Proof. Let $\left\{ f ^ { 1 } , \dotsc , f ^ { M _ { F } } \right\} \subseteq { \mathcal { F } } _ { h + 1 }$ be an η<sub>F</sub>-net and $\left\{ g ^ { 1 } , \ldots , g ^ { M _ { G } } \right\} \subseteq { \mathcal { G } } _ { h }$ be an η<sub>G</sub>-net, where $M _ { F } = \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \overset { \cdot } { \eta _ { F } } )$ and $M _ { G } \doteq \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } )$

Fix a net pair $( f ^ { r } , g ^ { m } )$ . Flatten the pooled stage-h samples $\mathcal { D } _ { h } ^ { ( 1 ) } , \dots , \mathcal { D } _ { h } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ into an arbitrary sequence indexed by $t \in [ N _ { h , < k } ]$ . For the t-th sample, define

$$
X _ { t } ^ { ( r , m ) } : = \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { r } ; s _ { t } , a _ { t } , b _ { t } , s _ { t } ^ { \prime } ; g ^ { m } ) - \mathbb { E } \big [ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { r } ; s _ { t } , a _ { t } , b _ { t } , s _ { t } ^ { \prime } ; g ^ { m } ) \mid \mathcal { F } _ { t - 1 } \big ] ,
$$

where $\mathcal { F } _ { t - 1 }$ is the history before revealing that sample. By construction, $\{ X _ { t } ^ { ( r , m ) } \} _ { t = 1 } ^ { N _ { h , < k } }$ is a martingale-difference sequence. Assumption 10 gives $| X _ { t } ^ { ( r , m ) } | \ \leqslant \ 2 B _ { \phi } ( \sigma )$ , and the conditional mean term equals the corresponding summand of $\bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } )$ . Azuma–Hoeffding therefore yields

$$
\mathbb { P } \bigg ( \big | \widehat { \mathcal L } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) - \bar { \mathcal L } _ { h , k } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) \big | > t \bigg ) \leqslant 2 \exp \left( - \frac { N _ { h , < k } t ^ { 2 } } { 2 B _ { \phi } ^ { 2 } ( \sigma ) } \right) .
$$

Applying a union bound over all $M _ { F } M _ { G }$ net pairs gives that with probability at least $1 - \delta .$

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \in [ M _ { F } ] , m \in [ M _ { G } ] } \big | \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) - \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) \big | \leqslant B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \frac { 2 \log \left( 2 M _ { F } M _ { G } / \delta \right) } { N _ { h , < k } } } .\tag{25}
$$

The Lipschitz extension from the finite net to the full class is standard. Specifically, for arbitrary $( f , g )$ and nearest net points $( { \bar { f } } , { \bar { g } } )$ , Assumption 11 implies

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { } & { \big \vert \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g ; f ) - \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \big \vert \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) , } \\ & { } & { \big \vert \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) - \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \big \vert \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) . } \end{array}
$$

Combining these two inequalities with (25) proves (24).

Corollary 22 (Pooled ERM excess risk). Under Assumptions $I O { - } I 2 ,$ , with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ simultaneously for all $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$

$$
\bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) \leqslant 2 \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) .\tag{26}
$$

Proof. Fix $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and $\tau > 0$ . Choose $g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h }$ such that

$$
\bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) \leqslant \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) + \tau .
$$

On the event (24),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) \leqslant \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) + \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p o o l } } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) + \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) + 2 \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant \displaystyle \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) + 2 \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \tau . } \end{array}
$$

Letting $\tau \downarrow 0$ proves (26).

Corollary 23 (Backward-validation operator transfer). Under Assumptions $I O { - } I 3 ,$ with probability at least $1 - \delta _ { \mathrm { { \scriptsize \cdot } } }$ , simultaneouslyfor all $\bar { f } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ ,

$$
\sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) ,\tag{27}
$$

where

$$
\rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) : = \frac { 2 B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) N _ { h , < k } } { n _ { h , \operatorname* { m i n } } ^ { ( k ) } } \Bigl ( 2 \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } \Bigr ) .
$$

Proof. Fix $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and define the pointwise operator gap

$$
\begin{array} { r } { e _ { h , k } ( f ) : = \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } . } \end{array}
$$

By the ambient dual representation (23), the function $- e _ { h , k } ( f )$ is pointwise nonnegative. Assumption 10 further implies

$$
- 2 B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \leqslant e _ { h , k } ( f ; s , a , b ) \leqslant 0 \qquad { \mathrm { f o r ~ a l l ~ } } ( s , a , b ) .\tag{28}
$$

Therefore

$$
\sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ e _ { h , k } ( f ) ^ { 2 } \right] = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \frac { N _ { h , < k } } { n _ { h , j } } \lambda _ { h , j } ^ { ( k ) } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ e _ { h , k } ( f ) ^ { 2 } \right]\tag{29}
$$

$$
\leqslant \frac { N _ { h , < k } } { n _ { h , \operatorname* { m i n } } ^ { ( k ) } } \mathbb { E } _ { \bar { \mu } _ { h , k } } \left[ e _ { h , k } ( f ) ^ { 2 } \right]\tag{30}
$$

$$
\leqslant \frac { 2 B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) N _ { h , < k } } { n _ { h , \operatorname* { m i n } } ^ { ( k ) } } \mathbb { E } _ { \bar { \mu } _ { h , k } } \left[ - e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right] ,\tag{31}
$$

where the last step uses (28).

Next,

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \bar { \mu } _ { h , k } } \left[ - e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right] = \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { \bar { g } \in \bar { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } } \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \bar { g } ; f ) .
$$

Adding and subtracting in $\mathrm { f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \bar { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f )$ and then applying Corollary 22 together with Assumption 13 yields

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } _ { \bar { \mu } _ { h , k } } \left[ - e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right] \leqslant 2 \bar { \Delta } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } . } \end{array}
$$

Substituting this bound into (31) proves (27).

Proposition 24 (Backward validation induced by the current confidence set). Fix a stage h and an episode $k \geqslant 2$ . On the intersection ofthe eventsfrom Lemma 20 and Corollary 23, ifthe confidence set update satisfies $\widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ( f ) \leqslant \beta _ { h , k }$ for every $f \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ , then every $f \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ obeys

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { e x a c t } } ( f ) \leqslant 2 \beta _ { h , k } + 2 \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) + 2 \rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) . } \end{array}\tag{32}
$$

Proof. Fix any $f \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ . The identity $\delta _ { h } ( f ) = \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ) + e _ { h , k } ( f )$ holds pointwise, so

$$
\begin{array} { l } { { \displaystyle \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { e x a c t } } ( f ) = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ) + e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] } \ ~ } \\ { { \displaystyle \leqslant 2 \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] + 2 \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] } \ ~ } \\ { { \displaystyle = 2 \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ) + 2 \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( e _ { h , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] } . } \end{array}
$$

On the event of Corollary 23, the second term is at most $2 \rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } )$ . On the event of Lemma 20,

$$
\mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ) \leqslant \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ( f ) + \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } + \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) ,
$$

where the last step uses the defining property of $\mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ . Substituting both bounds proves (32).

In particular, if the selected function $f ^ { ( k ) }$ satisfies $2 \beta _ { h , k } + 2 \xi _ { h , k } \mathopen { } \mathclose \bgroup \left( \delta ; \eta _ { W } \aftergroup \egroup \right) + 2 \rho _ { h , k } \mathopen { } \mathclose \bgroup \left( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } \aftergroup \egroup \right) \leqslant \epsilon ^ { 2 }$ then the exact witness $\psi _ { h } ^ { k } : = \delta _ { h } ( f ^ { ( k ) } )$ satisfies the backward-validation premise (18) needed by Lemma 17.

## C.4 Closing Optimism from the Confidence Set

Assumption 14 (Reference realizability for optimism closure). There exists a reference function $f ^ { \dagger } \in { \mathcal { F } }$ such that

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant V _ { f ^ { \dagger } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } }\tag{33}
$$

and, for every stage h,

$$
f _ { h } ^ { \dagger } = \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } .\tag{34}
$$

Proposition 25 (Exact robust realizability implies an optimism reference). Suppose there exists $f ^ { \star } \in \mathcal { F }$ such thatfor every stage $h \in [ H ]$

$$
f _ { h } ^ { \star } = { \mathcal { T } } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \star } \qquad a n d \qquad V _ { f ^ { \star } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) = V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) .
$$

Then Assumption 14 holds with $f ^ { \dagger } = f ^ { \star } a n d \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } = 0 .$

Proof. Take $f ^ { \dagger } : = f ^ { \star }$ . The fixed-point identity is exactly (34), and the value identity implies (33) with $\epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } = 0$ □

Proposition 26 (Reference retention). Under Assumption 14, on the intersection of the events from Lemma 20 and Corollary 23, if

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \beta _ { h , k } \geqslant \xi _ { h , k } \mathopen { } \mathclose \bgroup \left( \delta ; \eta _ { W } \aftergroup \egroup \right) + \rho _ { h , k } \mathopen { } \mathclose \bgroup \left( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } \aftergroup \egroup \right) \qquad f o r e \nu e r y ~ h \in [ H ] , } \end{array}\tag{35}
$$

then $f ^ { \dagger } \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$

Proof. Fix a stage h. By (34), the exact residual of $f ^ { \dagger }$ is zero: $\delta _ { h } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \equiv 0$ . Hence $\widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) =$ $- e _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } )$ pointwise, and therefore

$$
\mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( e _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } )
$$

on the event of Corollary 23. Applying Lemma 20 then gives

$$
\widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \leqslant \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) + \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \xi _ { h , k } ( \delta ; \eta _ { W } ) .
$$

By (35), the right-hand side is at most $\beta _ { h , k }$ . Since this holds for every stage h, the definition of $\mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ implies $f ^ { \dagger } \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ □

Corollary 27 (Approximate optimism from confidence sets). UnderAssumption 14 and the conditions of Proposition 26, the algorithm’s optimistic choice satisfies

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } .
$$

In particular, Assumption 5 is implied by the backward-validation confidence set construction.

Proof. By Proposition 26, $\begin{array} { r l r } { f ^ { \dagger } } & { { } \in } & { \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) } } \end{array}$ Since the algorithm chooses $f ^ { ( k ) } \in$ ar $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname { g m a x } _ { f \in \mathfrak { F } ^ { ( k - 1 ) } } V _ { f , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) } \end{array}$ , we have

$$
V _ { f ^ { ( k ) } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) \geqslant V _ { f ^ { \dagger } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) .
$$

Combining this inequality with (33) proves the claim.

Assumption 15 (Projected reference realizability for optimism closure). Fix stagewise maps $\{ \mathsf { P } _ { h } \} _ { h = 1 } ^ { H }$ There exists a referencefunction $f ^ { \dagger } \in { \mathcal { F } }$ such that

$$
V _ { 1 } ^ { \star , \sigma } ( s _ { 1 } ) \leqslant V _ { f ^ { \dagger } , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } }\tag{36}
$$

and, for every stage $h \in [ H ]$

$$
f _ { h } ^ { \dagger } = \mathsf { P } _ { h } \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } .\tag{37}
$$

Proposition 28 (Projected reference retention). Fix stagewise maps $\{ \mathsf { P } _ { h } \} _ { h = 1 } ^ { H }$ <sub>1</sub> and let

$$
\mathfrak { F } _ { \mathtt { P } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) } : = \left\{ f \in \mathcal { F } : \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathtt { P } } ( f ) \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathtt { P } } f o r e \nu e r y h \in [ H ] \right\} .\tag{38}
$$

For any $f \in { \mathcal { F } } ,$ , define the projected plug-in residual

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ) : = f _ { h } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { T } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ,
$$

the projected operator error

$$
\begin{array} { r } { e _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ) : = \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } , } \end{array}
$$

and the projected plug-in backward-validation score

$$
\operatorname { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \operatorname { P } , \operatorname { p l u g } } ( f ) : = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( \widehat { \delta } _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] .
$$

Under Assumption 15, if for every stage $h \in [ H ]$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \big | \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) - \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } , \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \big | \leqslant \xi _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } , } \end{array}\tag{39}
$$

$$
\sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( e _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ,\tag{40}
$$

$$
\beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } \geqslant \xi _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } + \rho _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ,\tag{41}
$$

then $f ^ { \dag } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathsf { P } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$

Proof. Fix a stage $h \in [ H ]$ . By (37), the projected exact residual of $f ^ { \dagger }$ is zero. Therefore

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) = - e _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } )
$$

pointwise, which implies

$$
\mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } , \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ \left( e _ { h , \mathsf { P } , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ^ { \mathsf { P } }
$$

by (40). Combining this with (39) gives

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \leqslant \mathrm { B V } _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } , \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) + \xi _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \leqslant \rho _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } } + \xi _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { P } } . } \end{array}
$$

Since this holds for every stage $h ,$ definition (38) implies $f ^ { \dag } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathsf { P } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$

Corollary 29 (Projected approximate optimism from confidence sets). Under Assumption 15 and the conditions ofProposition 28, ifthe algorithm chooses

$$
f ^ { ( k ) } \in \underset { f \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { P } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) } } { \mathrm { a r g m a x } } V _ { f , 1 } ( s _ { 1 } ) ,

Proof. By Proposition 28, $f ^ { \dag } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathsf { P } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ . Combining this with (36) proves the claim. □

## C.5 A Covering-Number Dual ERM Concentration Lemma

We now return to a fresh-sample, current-occupancy model to verify the on-policy operatorlearning budget Assumption 6. Fix a stage h and an occupancy measure $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ . Given i.i.d. samples $\{ ( s _ { i } , a _ { i } , b _ { i } , s _ { i } ^ { \prime } ) \} _ { i = 1 } ^ { N _ { h , k } }$ from the joint law $\mu _ { h } ^ { k } \otimes P _ { h } ^ { 0 }$ , define

$$
\widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) : = \frac { 1 } { N _ { h , k } } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N _ { h , k } } \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s _ { i } , a _ { i } , b _ { i } , s _ { i } ^ { \prime } ; g ) ,
$$

and let $\begin{array} { r } { \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } \in \operatorname { a r g m i n } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) } \end{array}$ . The fitted plug-in operator is

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \big ( \widehat { T } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } \big ) ( s , a , b ) : = r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) - \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } \big ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } \big ) \right] . } \end{array}
$$

Lemma 30 (Uniform dual concentration via covering numbers). Under Assumptions 10–12, for every $\delta \in ( 0 , 1 )$ , with probability at least $1 - \delta _ { : }$

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { f \in \mathscr { F } _ { h + 1 , g \in \mathscr { G } _ { h } } } \big | \widehat { \mathscr { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) - \mathscr { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) \big | \leqslant \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) ,\tag{42}
$$

where

$$
\Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) : = B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \frac { 2 \log \left( 2 \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F } ) \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } ) / \delta \right) } { N _ { h , k } } } + 2 L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) .
$$

Proof. Let $\left\{ f ^ { 1 } , \ldots , f ^ { M _ { F } } \right\} \subseteq { \mathcal { F } } _ { h + 1 }$ be an $\eta _ { F } { \bf - n e t }$ under $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 }$ and $\left\{ g ^ { 1 } , \ldots , g ^ { M _ { G } } \right\} \subseteq { \mathcal { G } } _ { h }$ be an η<sub>G</sub>-net under $d _ { \mathcal { G } , h }$ , where $\dot { M } _ { F } = \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F } )$ and $M _ { G } = \mathcal { N } _ { G } ( \eta _ { G } )$

Fix a net pair $( f ^ { r } , g ^ { m } )$ . By Assumption 10, the random variables $\ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { r } ; s _ { i } , a _ { i } , b _ { i } , s _ { i } ^ { \prime } ; g ^ { m } )$ are i.i.d. and take values in $[ - B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) , B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) ]$ . Hoeffding’s inequality therefore yields, for every $t > 0$

$$
\mathbb { P } \bigg ( \big | \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) \big | > t \bigg ) \leqslant 2 \exp \left( - \frac { N _ { h , k } t ^ { 2 } } { 2 B _ { \phi } ^ { 2 } ( \sigma ) } \right) .
$$

Applying a union bound over all $M _ { F } M _ { G }$ net pairs, we obtain that with probability at least $1 - \delta$

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { r \in [ M _ { F } ] , m \in [ M _ { G } ] } \big | \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ^ { m } ; f ^ { r } ) \big | \leqslant B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \frac { 2 \log \left( 2 M _ { F } M _ { G } / \delta \right) } { N _ { h , k } } } .\tag{43}
$$

Now fix arbitrary $( f , g ) \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } \times \mathcal { G } _ { h }$ , and choose net points $\bar { f } , \bar { g }$ such that $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f , \bar { f } ) \leqslant \eta _ { F }$ and $d _ { \mathcal { G } , h } ( g , \bar { g } ) \leqslant \eta _ { G }$ . By Assumption 11, for every sample $( s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } )$

$$
\big | \ell _ { \phi } \big ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g \big ) - \ell _ { \phi } \big ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \bar { g } \big ) \big | \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } \big ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } \big ) .
$$

Averaging this inequality over the empirical sample and over the population law separately gives

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { } & { \big \vert \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) - \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \big \vert \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) , } \\ & { } & { \big \vert \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \big \vert \leqslant L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) . } \end{array}
$$

Combining these two inequalities with (43) yields

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \left| \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) \right| \leqslant \left| \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g ; f ) - \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \right| + \left| \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) \right| } \\ & { \qquad + \left| \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \bar { g } ; \bar { f } ) - \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) \right| } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) \sqrt { \frac { 2 \log \left( 2 M _ { F } M _ { G } / \delta \right) } { N _ { h , k } } } + 2 L _ { \ell , h } ( \eta _ { F } + \eta _ { G } ) . } \end{array}
$$

Since $( f , g )$ was arbitrary, this proves (42).

Corollary 31 (ERM excess population risk). Under Assumptions 10–12, with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ , simultaneouslyfor all $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) \leqslant 2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) .\tag{44}
$$

Proof. Fix $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and $\tau > 0$ . Choose $g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h }$ such that

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) \leqslant \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) + \tau .
$$

On the event (42),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) \leqslant \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) + \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \leqslant \widehat { \mathcal { L } } _ { h , k } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) + \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \leqslant \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g _ { f , \tau } ^ { \star } ; f ) + 2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) } \\ & { \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \leqslant \displaystyle \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) + 2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \tau . } \end{array}
$$

Since $\tau > 0$ is arbitrary, (44) follows. The event (42) already holds uniformly over all $f ,$ so the conclusion is simultaneous in $f .$ □

Corollary 32 (Operator-learning budget from dual ERM). Under Assumptions $I O { - } I 3 ,$ with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ , simultaneouslyfor all $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant 2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } . } \end{array}\tag{45}
$$

Therefore Assumption 6 holds with $\alpha _ { h , k } = 2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } )$

Proof. Fix $f \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } . \mathrm { B y }$ (23), the pointwise operator gap is

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \bigl ( \widehat { T } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \bigr ) ( s , a , b ) = } & { \underset { \bar { g } \in \bar { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } } { \operatorname* { i n f } } \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \bar { g } ) \right] } \\ & { - \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ) \right] . } \end{array}
$$

Because the first term is a pointwise infimum, the right-hand side is nonpositive. Hence the $L _ { 1 } ( \mu _ { h } ^ { k } )$ norm equals the $\mu _ { h } ^ { k }$ -expectation of its negative:

$$
\Big \| \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \Big \| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } = \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { \bar { g } \in \bar { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \bar { g } ; f ) .\tag{46}
$$

Add and subtract i $\operatorname { t f } _ { g \in { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } } { \mathcal { L } } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f )$ in (46) to get

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \left\| \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } = \underbrace { \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \hat { g } _ { h , f } ^ { ( k ) } ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) } _ { \mathrm { E R M } \mathrm { e s t i m a t i o n ~ e r r o r } } } & { } \\ & { + \underbrace { \operatorname* { i n f } _ { g \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( g ; f ) - \operatorname* { i n f } _ { \bar { g } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } } \mathcal { L } _ { h } ^ { \mu } ( \bar { g } ; f ) } _ { \mathrm { d u a l a p p r o x i m a t i o n ~ e r r o r } } . } \end{array}
$$

The first term is bounded by $2 \Delta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { c o v } } ( \delta ; \eta _ { F } , \eta _ { G } )$ by Corollary 31; the second term is bounded by ϵ<sub>dual</sub> by Assumption 13. This proves (45). □

## C.6 Concrete Structural Upper Bounds

We now compress the witness and covering assumptions into explicit formulas for common model classes. Throughout this subsection, write $\bar { \mathcal { X } } _ { h } : = \bar { \mathcal { S } } \times \mathcal { A } \times \mathcal { B }$

Lemma 33 (Witness-envelope bound). Assume rewards satisfy $0 \leqslant r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) \leqslant 1$ and the value class is uniformly bounded at stage $h \colon \operatorname* { s u p } _ { f \in { \mathcal { F } } } \| f _ { h } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant B _ { F , h }$ . Under Assumption 10,

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { h } } \| w \| _ { \infty } \leqslant B _ { F , h } + 1 + B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) .
$$

Therefore Assumption 8 holds with $B _ { W , h } = B _ { F , h } + 1 + B _ { \phi } ( \sigma )$

Proof. Fix any $w _ { h , f , g } \in \mathcal { W } _ { h }$ . For every $( s , a , b ) \in \mathcal { X } _ { h }$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \left| w _ { h , f , g } ( s , a , b ) \right| \leqslant | f _ { h } ( s , a , b ) | + | r _ { h } ( s , a , b ) | + \left| \mathbb { E } _ { s ^ { \prime } \sim P _ { h } ^ { 0 } ( \cdot \vert s , a , b ) } \left[ \ell _ { \phi } ( f _ { h + 1 } ; s , a , b , s ^ { \prime } ; g ) \right] \right| } \\ & { \qquad \leqslant B _ { F , h } + 1 + B _ { \phi } ( \sigma ) , } \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality uses the reward bound and Assumption 10. Taking the supremum over $( f , g )$ proves the claim. □

Lemma 34 (Exact residual envelope under completeness). Assume robust minimax completeness holds stagewise and that the value class is uniformly bounded: s $\begin{array} { r } { \Psi _ { f \in \mathcal { F } } \| f _ { h } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant B _ { F , h } \bar { f } o r } \end{array}$ every $h \in [ H ]$ . Then every exact robust residual $\psi \in \mathcal { P } _ { h } = ( I - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } ) \mathcal { F }$ satisfies

$$
\| \psi \| _ { \infty } \leqslant 2 B _ { F , h } .
$$

Proof. Fix $\psi \in \mathcal { P } _ { h }$ . By definition, there exists $f \in { \mathcal { F } }$ such that $\psi = f _ { h } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 }$ . By robust minimax completeness, $\mathcal T _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal F _ { h }$ . Hence both $f _ { h }$ and $\mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 }$ have sup norm at most $B _ { F , h }$ , so

$$
\| \psi \| _ { \infty } \leqslant \| f _ { h } \| _ { \infty } + \| T _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \| _ { \infty } \leqslant 2 B _ { F , h } .
$$

Proposition 35 (Finite-dimensional linear covering bound). $\begin{array} { r l r l } { L e t } & { { } \ \mathcal { H } } & { } & { { } = } \end{array}$ $\left\{ x \stackrel { \cdot } { \mapsto } \langle \theta , \psi ( x ) \rangle : \theta \in { \mathbb { R } } ^ { d } , \| \theta \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R \right\}$ be a linear function class on a domain X , and assume $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname* { s u p } _ { x \in \mathcal { X } } \| \psi ( x ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant \kappa . } \end{array}$ . $$

Proof. Let $\Theta : = \big \{ \theta \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d } : \| \theta \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R \big \}$ . The Euclidean ball Θ admits an $\eta /$ κ-net of cardinality at most $( 1 + 2 R \kappa / \eta ) ^ { d }$ . For every pair of parameters $\theta , { \tilde { \theta } }$ and every $x \in \mathcal { X }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \big | \langle \theta , \psi ( x ) \rangle - \langle \tilde { \theta } , \psi ( x ) \rangle \big | \leqslant \| \theta - \tilde { \theta } \| _ { 2 } \| \psi ( x ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant \kappa \| \theta - \tilde { \theta } \| _ { 2 } . } \end{array}
$$

Hence an $\eta / \kappa$ -cover in parameter space induces an η-cover of H under the sup norm. Taking logarithms yields (47). □
