\Pr ( E _ { j } = 1 \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \geqslant q _ { \exp , h } , \qquad q _ { \exp , h } > 0 ,\tag{103}
$$

and whenever $E _ { j } = 1$ the episode is rolled in with an explorer pair whose stage-h state marginal $d _ { h , \mathrm { e x p } } ^ { j }$ satisfies

$$
d _ { h , \mathrm { e x p } } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) \geqslant \rho _ { h } .\tag{104}
$$

Then (101) holds with $\rho _ { h }$ replaced by $q _ { \mathrm { e x p } , h } \rho _ { h }$ , and therefore

$$
\bar { \omega } _ { h } = q _ { \mathrm { e x p } , h } \rho _ { h } \omega _ { h , \mathrm { g o o d } }\tag{105}
$$

is a valid scalar design-value lower bound.

Proof. Condition on $\mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 }$ . By the law of total expectation and the nonnegativity of conditional probabilities,

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) = \operatorname* { P r } ( E _ { j } = 1 \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } , E _ { j } = 1 ) } & { } \\ { + \operatorname* { P r } ( E _ { j } = 0 \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } , E _ { j } = 0 ) } & { } \\ { \geqslant \operatorname* { P r } ( E _ { j } = 1 \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } , E _ { j } = 1 ) . } & { } \end{array}
$$

Under the explorer branch, $\operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } \mid { \mathcal { H } } _ { j - 1 } , E _ { j } = 1 ) = d _ { h , \exp } ^ { j } ( { \mathcal { G } } _ { h } ) \geqslant \rho _ { h }$ by (104), while (103) gives $\operatorname* { P r } ( E _ { j } = 1 \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \geqslant q _ { \exp , h }$ . Hence

$$
\operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \geqslant q _ { \exp , h } \rho _ { h } .
$$

This is exactly (101) with $\rho _ { h }$ replaced by $q _ { \mathrm { e x p } , h } \rho _ { h }$ . Applying Proposition 68 proves (105). □

Proposition 70 (Existing exploiter-mix implies reachability from BR component dynamics). Fix a stage $h \in \left[ H \right]$ and an epoch $e \in [ M ]$ . Assume the executed pair in every pre-epoch episode $j \in \cup _ { r < e } \mathcal { E } _ { r }$ is drawn according to the existing episode-level exploiter-mix rule used in Proposition $5 l { : }$ with conditional probabilities $1 - p , p / 2 ,$ and $p / 2 ,$ , the episode executes one of $( \pi ^ { j } , \nu ^ { j } )$ $( \mathrm { B R } _ { 1 } ^ { j } , \nu ^ { j } )$ , and $( \pi ^ { j } , \mathrm { B R } _ { 2 } ^ { j } )$ , respectively. Let $d _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } ^ { j } , d _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } ^ { j } , d _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } ^ { j }$ denote the corresponding stage-h state marginals under the interaction process. Suppose there exists a measurable good-state set $\mathcal { G } _ { h } \subseteq S$ such that,for every pre-epoch episode j,

$$
d _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) \geqslant \rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } , \qquad d _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) \geqslant \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } , \qquad d _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) \geqslant \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } ,\tag{106}
$$

with deterministic nonnegative constants $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } , \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } , \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } .$ . Then the stage-h state ofthe actually executed episode satisfies

$$
\operatorname* { P r } \biggr ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } \biggl ) \geqslant ( 1 - p ) \rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 1 } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 2 } .\tag{107}
$$

Ifin addition $\omega _ { h } ( s ) \geqslant \omega _ { h , \mathrm { g o o d } }$ for all $s \in \mathcal G _ { h }$ , then Proposition 68 applies with

$$
\bar { \omega } _ { h } = \Big ( ( 1 - p ) \rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 1 } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 2 } \Big ) \omega _ { h , \mathrm { g o o d } } .
$$

Proof. Condition on $\mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 }$ . Let $B _ { j } \in \{ \mathrm { t a r } , \mathrm { b r } 1 , \mathrm { b r } 2 \}$ denote the branch selected by the exploiter-mix rule in episode j. By the law of total probability,

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) = } & { \displaystyle \sum _ { b \in \{ \operatorname { t a r } , \mathrm { b r } ^ { 1 } , \mathrm { b r } ^ { 2 } \} } \operatorname* { P r } ( B _ { j } = b \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ) \operatorname* { P r } ( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } , B _ { j } = b ) } \\ & { = ( 1 - p ) d _ { h , \operatorname { t a r } } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) + \displaystyle \frac { p } { 2 } d _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 1 } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) + \displaystyle \frac { p } { 2 } d _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 2 } ^ { j } ( \mathcal { G } _ { h } ) . } \end{array}
$$

Using (106) yields (107). The design-value claim then follows from Proposition 68.

Corollary 71 (BR-only reachability lower bound). Under the assumptions of Proposition 70, if no lower bound is assumedfor the target branch but both BR branches satisfy $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } , \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } \geqslant \rho _ { h , }$ BR for some $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { B R } } > 0$ , then

$$
\operatorname* { P r } \left( s _ { h } ^ { j } \in \mathcal { G } _ { h } \mid \mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } \right) \geqslant p \rho _ { h , \mathrm { B R } }
$$

and therefore

$$
\bar { \omega } _ { h } \geqslant p \rho _ { h , \mathrm { B R } } \omega _ { h , \mathrm { g o o d } } .
$$

Proof. Set $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } = 0$ in Proposition 70 and use $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 1 } } , \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r 2 } } \geqslant \rho _ { h , \mathrm { B R } } .$

Primitive BR-mix coverage from reachability and local nondegeneracy. Assume finite action spaces and the explicit smoothing rule (81) with the uniform action-pair design $u _ { h } ^ { \mathrm { u n i f } }$ from Corollary 67. Fix a stage $h \in [ H ]$ and an epoch $e \in [ M ]$ . Suppose there exists a measurable good-state set $\mathcal { G } _ { h } \subseteq S .$ , a stagewise local nondegeneracy constant $s _ { h } > 0 ;$ , and branch-mass constants $\rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } , \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 1 } , \rho _ { h }$ ,br2 $\geqslant 0$ such that:

(i) for every $s \in \mathcal G _ { h }$

$$
\lambda _ { \operatorname* { m i n } } \bigl ( \Phi _ { h } ( s ) \Phi _ { h } ( s ) ^ { \intercal } \bigr ) \geqslant s _ { h } ^ { 2 } ,\tag{108}
$$

where $\Phi _ { h } ( s )$ is the witness-feature matrix from (98);

(ii) the current BR-based exploiter-mix obeys the branch good-state lower bounds (106).

Define

$$
\alpha _ { h } : = ( 1 - p ) \rho _ { h , \mathrm { t a r } } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 1 } + \frac { p } { 2 } \rho _ { h , \mathrm { b r } 2 } .\tag{109}
$$

Then the predictable excitation budget satisfies

$$
\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } \geqslant \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \frac { \alpha _ { h } s _ { h } ^ { 2 } } { | \boldsymbol { A } | | \boldsymbol { B } | } N _ { e } ^ { - } .\tag{110}
$$

Consequently, the predictable-growth premise (132) of Corollary 79 holds with

$$
\underline { { \nu } } _ { h } = \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \ : \frac { \alpha _ { h } s _ { h } ^ { 2 } } { | \boldsymbol { A } | | \boldsymbol { B } | } .\tag{111}
$$

In the BR-only case of Corollary $7 1 , \alpha _ { h }$ can be replaced by $p \rho _ { h , \mathrm { B R } }$ . This follows because (108) and (98) imply $\omega _ { h } ( s ) \geqslant s _ { h } ^ { 2 } / ( | { \mathcal { A } } | | B | )$ for every $s \in \mathcal G _ { h }$ . Proposition 70 and Proposition 68 then yield $\bar { \omega } _ { h } = \alpha _ { h } s _ { h } ^ { 2 } / ( | \mathcal { A } | | B | )$ , and Proposition 66 converts this scalar design-value lower bound into (110) and (111).

Assumption 20 (Uniform ridge-linear dual specialization over the linear value class). For each stage $h \in [ H ] ,$ , consider thefull linear value class $\mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ equipped with the metric $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 }$ from Assumption ${ \dot { I } } I .$ For every epoch e, stage $h ,$ and continuation function $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ , there exists a parameter $\vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { W , \hat { h } } }$ with $\| \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant S _ { G , h }$ and scalar pseudo-labels $\{ Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \} _ { j \in \cup _ { u < e } \mathcal { E } _ { u } }$ such that

$$
Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) = \left. \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) , \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \right. + \eta _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) ,\tag{112}
$$

where $\eta _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } )$ is conditionally mean zero and R -sub-Gaussian given $\mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 }$ . Assume moreover that the pseudo-label and target-parameter maps are uniformly Lipschitz:

$$
\left| Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - Y _ { h , j } ( \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \right| \leqslant L _ { Y , h } d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } , \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } ) ,\tag{113}
$$

$$
\| \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant L _ { \star , h } d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } , \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } )\tag{114}
$$

for all $f _ { h + 1 } , \tilde { f } _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ . The epochwise dual refit is the ridge estimator

$$
\widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \in \underset { \vartheta \in \mathbb R ^ { d _ { W , h } } } { \mathrm { a r g m i n } } \left. \sum _ { j \in \cup _ { u < c } \varepsilon _ { u } } \left( Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \langle \vartheta , \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \rangle \right) ^ { 2 } + \lambda \| \vartheta \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \right. ,\tag{115}
$$

and the induced operator gap satisfies

$$
\bigl ( \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \bigr ) ( x ) = \Bigl \langle \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) , \phi _ { W , h } ( x ) \Bigr \rangle .\tag{116}
$$

Proposition 72 (Affine continuation coupling removes separate Lipschitz inputs). Fix a stage $h \in [ H ]$ and suppose the continuation class admits the parameterization

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } = \left\{ f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } : x \mapsto \langle \alpha , \phi _ { F , h + 1 } ( x ) \rangle : \alpha \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { F , h } } , \| { \alpha } \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R _ { F , h } \right\} . } \end{array}
$$

Assume that for every pre-epoch sample index $j \in \cup _ { u < e } \mathcal { E } _ { u } ,$ , there exist a scalar $y _ { h , j } ^ { 0 } ,$ a vector $a _ { h , j } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { F , h } }$ , a parameter $\vartheta _ { h , 0 } ^ { \star } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { W , h } }$ , and a matrix $B _ { h } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { W , h } \times d _ { F , h } }$ such that

$$
Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } ) = y _ { h , j } ^ { 0 } + \langle a _ { h , j } , \alpha \rangle + \eta _ { h , j } ( \alpha ) ,\tag{117}
$$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } ) = \vartheta _ { h , 0 } ^ { \star } + B _ { h } \alpha , } \end{array}\tag{118}
$$

where $\eta _ { h , j } ( \alpha )$ is conditionally mean zero and $R _ { G , h }$ -sub-Gaussian given $\mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 }$ for every α. If

$$
\operatorname* { s u p } _ { j \in \cup _ { u < e } \mathcal { E } _ { u } } \| a _ { h , j } \| _ { 2 } \leqslant A _ { Y , h } , \qquad \| B _ { h } \| _ { \mathrm { o p } } \leqslant A _ { \star , h } ,
$$

then Assumption 20 holds with the parameter metric $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } , f _ { h + 1 , \tilde { \alpha } } ) : = \| \alpha - \tilde { \alpha } \| _ { 2 } ,$ , the radius choice $S _ { G , h } = \| \vartheta _ { h , 0 } ^ { \star } \| _ { 2 } + A _ { \star , h } ^ { - } R _ { F , h }$ , and the derived continuity constants $L _ { Y , h } = A _ { Y , h }$ and $L _ { \star , h } = A _ { \star , h }$

Proof. Fix any $\alpha , \tilde { \alpha } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { F , h } }$ with $\| \alpha \| _ { 2 } , \| \tilde { \alpha } \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R _ { F , h }$ . By (118),

$$
\| \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant \| \vartheta _ { h , 0 } ^ { \star } \| _ { 2 } + \| B _ { h } \| _ { \mathrm { o p } } \| \alpha \| _ { 2 } \leqslant \| \vartheta _ { h , 0 } ^ { \star } \| _ { 2 } + A _ { \star , h } R _ { F , h } = S _ { G , h } .
$$

Also, (117) gives

$$
\left| Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } ) - Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 , { \tilde { \alpha } } } ) \right| = \left| \langle a _ { h , j } , \alpha - { \tilde { \alpha } } \rangle \right| \leqslant A _ { Y , h } \| \alpha - { \tilde { \alpha } } \| _ { 2 } ,
$$

while (118) yields

$$
\| \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 , \alpha } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 , \tilde { \alpha } } ) \| _ { 2 } = \| B _ { h } ( \alpha - \tilde { \alpha } ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant A _ { \star , h } \| \alpha - \tilde { \alpha } \| _ { 2 } .
$$

These are exactly (113) and (114) under the parameter metric. The remaining parts of Assumption 20 are already built into (117)–(118). □

Proposition 73 (Uniform self-normalized ridge control over the value class). Assume Assumption 20. Then with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ , simultaneously for all stages $h \in [ H ]$ , epochs $e \in [ \bar { M } ]$ , and continuationfunctions $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \right\| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } } \leqslant \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) , } \end{array}\tag{119}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) } & { : = R _ { G , h } \sqrt { 2 d _ { W , h } \log \left( 1 + \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } N _ { e } ^ { - } } { \lambda } \right) + 2 \log \left( \frac { 2 H M \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ) } { \delta } \right) } } \\ & { \qquad + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } + L _ { Y , h } + ( \sqrt { \lambda } + \kappa _ { W , h } ) L _ { \star , h } , } \end{array}\tag{120}
$$

and $\eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } : = ( N _ { e } ^ { - } \vee 1 ) ^ { - 1 / 2 }$

Proof. Fix a stage $h ,$ an epoch $e ,$ and let $\mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } ^ { \sharp , e } \subseteq \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ be an $\eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } }$ -net under $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 }$ with cardinality at most $\displaystyle \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } )$ . Fix a net point $\bar { f } _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } ^ { \sharp , e }$ . By Assumption 20, the pre-epoch samples satisfy the standard linear regression model (112) with design vectors $\phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } )$ , parameter $\vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } \big ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } \big )$ , and conditionally $R _ { G , h }$ -sub-Gaussian noise. Applying the standard self-normalized bound for ridge regression to the estimator (115) yields, with probability at least $1 - \delta ^ { \prime }$

$$
\left\| \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \right\| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } } \leqslant R _ { G , h } \sqrt { 2 \log \left( \frac { \operatorname* { d e t } ( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } ) ^ { 1 / 2 } } { \delta ^ { \prime } \operatorname* { d e t } ( \lambda I _ { d _ { W , h } } ) ^ { 1 / 2 } } \right) } + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } .
$$

Because $\Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \preceq ( \lambda + \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } N _ { e } ^ { - } ) I _ { d _ { W , h } }$ , we have

$$
\log \frac { \operatorname * { d e t } ( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } ) ^ { 1 / 2 } } { \operatorname * { d e t } ( \lambda I _ { d _ { W , h } } ) ^ { 1 / 2 } } \leqslant \frac { d _ { W , h } } { 2 } \log \left( 1 + \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } N _ { e } ^ { - } } { \lambda } \right) .
$$

Choosing $\delta ^ { \prime } = \delta / ( H M \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ) )$ and union bounding over all stages, epochs, and net points gives the stated bound simultaneously for every $\bar { f } _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } ^ { \sharp , e }$

Now fix an arbitrary $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ and choose $\bar { f } _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } ^ { \sharp , e }$ such that $d _ { \mathcal { F } , h + 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } , \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \leqslant \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } }$ By the ridge normal equations,

$$
\widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) = \left( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \right) ^ { - 1 } \sum _ { j \in \cup _ { u < e } \mathscr { E } _ { u } } \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) ,
$$

so

$$
\widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) = \big ( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ) ^ { - 1 } \sum _ { j \in \cup _ { u < e } \mathcal { E } _ { u } } \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \Big ( Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - Y _ { h , j } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \Big ) .
$$

Let $\Phi _ { W , h , e } ^ { - }$ be the design matrix whose rows are $\phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) ^ { \top }$ . Then

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left. \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \right. _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } } ^ { 2 } = \Delta y ^ { \top } \Phi _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ) ^ { - 1 } \big ( \Phi _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ) ^ { \top } \Delta y \leqslant \| \Delta y \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } , } \end{array}
$$

where $\Delta y _ { j } : = Y _ { h , j } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - Y _ { h , j } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } )$ , and the last step uses $\Phi _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ( \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ) ^ { - 1 } \big ( \Phi _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \big ) ^ { \top } \preceq I .$ By (113),

$$
\| \Delta y \| _ { 2 } \leqslant L _ { Y , h } \sqrt { N _ { e } ^ { - } } \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \leqslant L _ { Y , h } .
$$

Also, by (114),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \left\| { \vartheta } _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - { \vartheta } _ { h } ^ { \star } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \right\| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } } \leqslant \left\| { \vartheta } _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - { \vartheta } _ { h } ^ { \star } ( \bar { f } _ { h + 1 } ) \right\| _ { 2 } \sqrt { \| \Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \| _ { \mathrm { o p } } } } & { } \\ { \leqslant L _ { \star , h } \eta _ { F , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \sqrt { \lambda + \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } N _ { e } ^ { - } } } & { } \\ { \leqslant ( \sqrt \lambda + \kappa _ { W , h } ) L _ { \star , h } . } \end{array}
$$

Combining these two displays with the net-point self-normalized bound and the triangle inequality proves (119) with (120). □

Corollary 74 (Ridge operator transfer under feature excitation). Assume Assumption 20 and the warm-start condition (89). Then with probability at least $1 - \delta$ , simultaneously for all stages $h \in [ H ]$ epochs $e \in [ M ]$ , continuationfunctions $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ , and executed episodes $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \widehat { T } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) , } \end{array}\tag{121}
$$

where

$$
\bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) : = \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } } { \sqrt { \lambda + \nu _ { h } N _ { e } ^ { - } } } \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) , \qquad \nu _ { h } : = \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \omega _ { h } / 2 .
$$

Proof. Under (89), Proposition 63 gives the Gram event $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { G r a m } } ( \nu _ { h } )$ with $\nu _ { h } = \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \omega _ { h } / 2$ . Assumption 20 identifies the operator-gap parameter in (116), and Proposition 73 controls that parameter in the pre-epoch Gram norm. Applying Corollary 62 with $\vartheta _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) = \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } )$ therefore yields (121). 口

Corollary 75 (Warm-start-free ridge operator transfer under predictable excitation). Assume Assumption 20 and the conditions ofProposition 64. Then with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ simultaneouslyfor all stages $h \in [ H ]$ , epochs $e \in [ M ]$ , continuationfunctions $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ , and executed episodes $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \widehat { T } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } ( \delta ) , } \end{array}\tag{122}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } ( \delta ) : = \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } } { \sqrt { \underline { { \Lambda } } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } ( \delta ) } } \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } ( \delta ) , } \\ & { \underline { { \Lambda } } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } ( \delta ) : = \lambda + \left[ \Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } - 4 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { N _ { e } ^ { - } \log \left( 2 d _ { W , h } H M / \delta \right) } \right] _ { + } . } \end{array}
$$

Proof. Proposition 64 implies $\Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \succeq \underline { { \Lambda } } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } ( \delta ) I _ { d _ { W , h } }$ on the stated high-probability event. Combining this with Proposition 73 and Corollary 62 gives (122). □

Proposition 76 (Projected operator transfer with an approximation remainder). Fix a stage $h \in [ H ]$ an epoch $e \in [ M ] ,$ , and a continuation function $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ . Let $\mathsf { P } _ { h } : L _ { \infty } ( S \times \bar { \mathcal { A } } \times \mathcal { B } ) \stackrel { \cdot } {  }$ $L _ { \infty } ( \boldsymbol { S } \times \mathcal { A } \times \dot { \boldsymbol { B } } )$ be such that,for every executed episode $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$

$$
\| \mathsf { P } _ { h } u - \mathsf { P } _ { h } v \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \| u - v \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \qquad f o r { a l l } u , v .\tag{123}
$$

Suppose the stagewise operator gap admits the decomposition

$$
\widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } = \zeta _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) + r _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) ,\tag{124}
$$

and thatfor every executed episode $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } ,$

$$
\| \zeta _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } , \qquad \| r _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \epsilon _ { \mathrm { o p , p r o j } } .\tag{125}
$$

Then

$$
\left\| \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { T } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { o p , p r o j } } \qquad f o r e \nu e r y \ k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } .\tag{126}
$$

Proof. Fix an executed episode $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ . By (123),

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \left\| \mathsf { P } _ { h } \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - \mathsf { P } _ { h } { \mathcal { T } } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \left\| \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - { \mathcal { T } } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } } & { } \\ { \leqslant \| \zeta _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } + \| r _ { h , e } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } } & { } \\ { \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { o p p r o j } } , } & { } \end{array}
$$

where the second step uses (124) and the triangle inequality, and the last step uses (125). This proves (126). □

Proposition 77 (Epochwise / cross-fit BV calibration for linear classes). Assume support control, Assumption 14, Assumption 18, Assumption 20, and the warm-start condition (89). Consider the epochwise / cross-fit variant described above, and let $\mathfrak { F } _ { \epsilon }$ denote the confidence set certified for deployment on epoch e. Define

$$
\Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \Big [ R _ { G , h } + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } + L _ { Y , h } + \big ( \sqrt { \lambda } + \kappa _ { W , h } \big ) L _ { \star , h } \Big ] , \qquad \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { h \in [ H ] } \frac { \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \omega _ { h } } { 2 } ,
$$

and

$$
B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } : = B _ { F } + \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } .
$$

Then there exist stagewise radii $\beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ depending only on the pre-epoch sample size $N _ { e } ^ { - }$ , the linear dimensions, and logarithmic confidence terms such that, with probability at least $1 - \delta$ , simultaneously for all epochs $e \in [ M ]$ and all stages $h \in [ H ]$

1. the optimism reference function is retained in $\mathfrak { F } _ { e }$

2. every function deployed on epoch e satisfies an exact residual budget of order

$$
\sum _ { k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left( \delta _ { h } ^ { k } \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( \left( B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \right) ^ { 2 } d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \left( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \right) \right) ;\tag{127}
$$

3. consequently Assumptions 5 and 7 hold for the deployed epochwise sequence with

$$
\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { e p - l i n } } ( K ) \leqslant \widetilde O \Bigl ( H \bigl ( B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \bigr ) ^ { 2 } d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) \log K \Bigr ) .\tag{128}
$$

Proof. Fix a stage h and an epoch e. Let $\mathcal { H } _ { e } ^ { - }$ denote the sigma-field generated by all samples collected before epoch e. By construction of the epochwise / cross-fit scheme, the pre-epoch dual refit and the induced plug-in witness family are $\mathcal { H } _ { e } ^ { - }$ -measurable because they are computed only from pre-epoch data. For every $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ , let $\widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , e , k } , \mathrm { B V } _ { h , e , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } , \mathrm { B V } _ { h , e , k } ^ { \mathrm { e x a c t } }$ denote the epoch-local backward-validation statistics obtained by restricting the sums in $( 2 1 ) ‐ ( 3 2 )$ to the fresh prefix $\{ j \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } : j < k \}$ and by using the frozen epoch-e plug-in operator.

Conditional on $\mathcal { H } _ { e } ^ { - }$ , the only randomness in these epoch-local statistics comes from fresh withinepoch samples. The proof of Lemma 20 can therefore be replayed after restricting the sample index set to the fresh epoch block. Concretely, fix $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ with nonempty fresh prefix $\mathcal { T } _ { e , k } : = \{ j \in \mathbf { \dot { \mathcal { E } } } _ { e } : j < k \}$ and let $\{ w ^ { 1 } , \bar { \ldots } , w ^ { M } \}$ be an η<sub>W,e</sub>-net of the frozen epoch-e witness class under $\| \cdot \| _ { \infty }$ . For a fixed net witness $w ^ { m }$ and each $j \in \mathcal { I } _ { e , k }$ , define

$$
Y _ { j } ^ { ( m , k ) } : = \frac { \left( w ^ { m } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \right) ^ { 2 } - \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( w ^ { m } ) ^ { 2 } \right] } { | \mathcal { J } _ { e , k } | } ,
$$

where $X _ { h } ^ { j } = ( S _ { h } ^ { j } , A _ { h } ^ { j } , B _ { h } ^ { j } )$ is the realized stage-h tuple in episode $j .$ Because the frozen witness family is $\mathcal { H } _ { e } ^ { - }$ -measurable and the stage-h sample of episode $j$ is drawn from $\mu _ { h } ^ { j }$ , we have $\mathbb { E } [ Y _ { j } ^ { ( m , k ) } \mid$ $\mathcal { H } _ { j - 1 } ] = 0$ and $| Y _ { j } ^ { ( m , k ) } | \leqslant B _ { F } ^ { 2 } / | { \mathcal { I } } _ { e , k } |$ . Azuma–Hoeffding therefore gives

$$
\mathbb { P } ( | \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { I } _ { e , k } } Y _ { j } ^ { ( m , k ) } | > t | \mathcal { H } _ { e } ^ { - } ) \leqslant 2 \exp ( - \frac { | \mathcal { I } _ { e , k } | t ^ { 2 } } { 2 B _ { F } ^ { 4 } } ) .
$$

Union bounding over the net witnesses, and then over the at most $m _ { e }$ choices of $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ , shows that with the stated confidence allocation,

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { k \in \mathcal { E } _ { c } } \operatorname* { m a x } _ { m \in [ M ] } \left. \frac { 1 } { \lvert \mathcal { T } _ { e , k } \rvert } \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { I } _ { c , k } } \left( w ^ { m } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \right) ^ { 2 } - \frac { 1 } { \lvert \mathcal { T } _ { e , k } \rvert } \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { I } _ { c , k } } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( w ^ { m } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \right. \lesssim B _ { F } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { \frac { \log \left( M m _ { e } / \delta \right) } { m _ { e } } } .
$$

For an arbitrary frozen witness $w ,$ choose a net point $\bar { w }$ with $\lVert w - \bar { w } \rVert _ { \infty } \leqslant \eta _ { W , e }$ . Exactly as in Lemma 20, $\lVert \dot { w ^ { 2 } } - \bar { w } ^ { 2 } \rVert _ { \infty } \leqslant 2 B _ { F } \eta _ { W , e } .$ , so both the empirical averages and the population averages

differ by at most $2 B _ { F } \eta _ { W , e }$ . Taking $\eta _ { W , e } \asymp m _ { e } ^ { - 1 / 2 }$ and using Corollary 36 to bound log M by $\widetilde { \cal O } \big ( d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K ) \big )$ yields an event $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { B V } }$ on which, simultaneously for all $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ and $f \in { \mathcal { F } }$

$$
\bigl | \widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , e , k } ( f ) - \mathrm { B V } _ { h , e , k } ^ { \mathrm { p l u g } } ( f ) \bigr | \leqslant \bar { \xi } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } , \qquad \bar { \xi } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } = \widetilde { O } \left( B _ { F } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { \frac { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) } { m _ { e } } } \right) .
$$

Next, the pre-epoch dual fit is based on $N _ { e } ^ { - }$ episodes. Proposition 73 and Corollary 74 give an event $\Omega _ { h , \epsilon } ^ { \mathrm { o p } }$ on which, simultaneously for all $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ and $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$ ,

$$
\left\| \widehat { T } _ { h , f _ { h + 1 } } ^ { \sigma , ( e ) } f _ { h + 1 } - T _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } \right\| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } , \qquad \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } = \widetilde { \mathcal { O } } \left( \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { \frac { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \left( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \right) } { N _ { e } ^ { - } } } \right) .
$$

Because the epoch schedule is geometric, $m _ { e } \asymp N _ { e } ^ { - }$ , so these two radii are of the same order up to constants and logarithms. Allocate confidence $\delta / ( 2 H M )$ to each of the events $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { B V } }$ and $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { o p } ^ { \bullet } }$ the logarithmic factors in the two radii absorb this choice, and a union bound over all $h \in [ H ]$ and $e \in [ \breve { M } ]$ produces a global event of probability at least $1 - \delta$ on which all displays below hold simultaneously.

Choose $\beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ to satisfy $\beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } \geqslant \bar { \xi } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } + \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ . On $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { B V } } \cap \Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { o p } }$ , every deployed $f \in \mathfrak { F } _ { e }$ satisfies the epoch-local confidence inequality

$$
\widehat { \mathrm { B V } } _ { h , e , k } ( f ) \leqslant \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } \qquad \mathrm { f o r } \mathrm { e v e r y } k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } ,
$$

because $\mathfrak { F } _ { e }$ is exactly the confidence set certified for deployment on epoch e using the fresh block $\mathcal { E } _ { e }$ . Proposition 24 applied to the epoch-local statistics implies that every candidate admitted by the epoch-e confidence update, and in particular every deployed function on epoch e, satisfies

$$
\mathrm { B V } _ { h , e , k } ^ { \mathrm { e x a c t } } ( f ) \leqslant 2 \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } + 2 \bar { \xi } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } + 2 \bar { \rho } _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } \lesssim \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } \qquad \mathrm { ~ f o r ~ a l l ~ } k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } .
$$

The same epoch-local application of Proposition 26 retains the Bellman-consistent reference function, so $f ^ { \dagger } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \epsilon }$ , which proves item 1.

The previous display is exactly the backward-validation premise (18) on the epoch-restricted deployed sequence, with a threshold of order $\beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } } .$ . Equivalently, for a constant $C > 0$ , we may set $\dot { \epsilon } _ { h , e } ^ { 2 } : = \begin{array} { r l } \end{array}$ $C \beta _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ and obtain $\begin{array} { r } { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } : j < k } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { j } } \left[ ( \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \epsilon _ { h , e } ^ { 2 } } \end{array}$ for every deployed $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ . Corollary 38 bounds the square-loss robust MG-BE dimension by $\widetilde { O } ( d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ) .$ , and the linear envelope bound is $B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } }$ Therefore Corollary 18 applied on the restricted epoch block gives

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \displaystyle \sum _ { k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e } } \mathbb { E } _ { \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ \left( \delta _ { h } ^ { k } \right) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( \left( B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \right) ^ { 2 } d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \left( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \right) \right) , } \end{array}
$$

which is exactly (127) and proves item 2.

Summing the last display over H stages and over the $M = { \cal { O } } ( \log K )$ epochs of the geometric schedule proves (128). Finally, on $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { o p } }$ we have $\widehat { \delta } _ { h } ^ { k } = \delta _ { h } ^ { k } - e _ { h } ^ { k }$ with $\| e _ { h } ^ { k } \| _ { 1 , \mu _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \bar { \rho } _ { h , \epsilon } ^ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ for all $k \in \mathcal { E } _ { e }$ so the plug-in residual budget has the same order by the same $( a + b ) ^ { 2 } \leqslant \ddot { 2 } a ^ { 2 } + 2 b ^ { 2 }$ algebra used earlier. This is exactly Assumption 7 for the deployed epochwise sequence, proving item 3. □

Theorem 78 (Formal epochwise / cross-fit linear theorem). Assume support control, stable stagegame extraction, and the epochwise / cross-fit variantjust described. Supposefurther that Assumptions 14, 18, and 20 hold, and that the explicit excitation schedule satisfies (81), (84), and the warm-start condition (89). In particular, by Proposition 58 and Corollary 59, robust minimax completeness and residual inclusion are concretely verified for TV-linear-mixture models with augmented linear features, while Proposition 63 and Corollary 74 replace the previous occupancy-ratio constant by a feature-excitation / self-normalized operator budget. Let

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \displaystyle { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ d _ { F , h } , d _ { G , h } , d _ { W , h } \right\} } , } \\ & { \displaystyle { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ \kappa _ { F , h } , \kappa _ { G , h } , \kappa _ { W , h } \right\} , } } \\ & { \displaystyle { R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ R _ { F , h } , R _ { G , h } , R _ { W , h } \right\} , } } \\ & { \displaystyle { B _ { F } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } B _ { F , h } , } } \\ & { \displaystyle { \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ R _ { G , h } + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } + L _ { Y , h } + ( \sqrt { \lambda } + \kappa _ { W , h } ) L _ { \star , h } \right\} , } } \\ & { \displaystyle { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { h \in [ H ] } \frac { \gamma _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \omega _ { h } } { 2 } . } } \end{array}
$$

Also define

$$
B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } : = B _ { F } + \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } .
$$

Then the target-policy regret sequence generated by the epochwise / cross-fit adaptive exploiter-mix algorithm satisfies

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant \displaystyle \frac { 1 } { 1 - p } \bigg [ \widetilde O \bigg ( H B _ { F } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) } \bigg ) } \\ & { \qquad + \widetilde O \bigg ( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) } \bigg ) } \\ & { \qquad + K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + p H K \bigg ] . } \end{array}\tag{129}
$$

Theorem 78 is therefore closed relative to Assumption 20; Proposition 72 supplies a primitive sufficient condition via affine continuation coupling, but TV-linear-mixture closure alone does not imply that operator-learning hypothesis.

Proof. Proposition 77 verifies both approximate optimism and the residual budget for the epochwise deployed sequence. In particular, Assumption 7 holds with $\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ( K ) = \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { e p - l i n } } ( K )$ , so Theorem 10 contributes the residual term

$$
\sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { e p - l i n } } ( K ) } \leqslant \widetilde O \left( H B _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { \mathrm { r i d g e } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K ) } \right) ,\tag{130}
$$

where the extra epoching factor is hidden in ${ \widetilde { O } } ( \cdot )$

For the operator-learning term, Corollary 74 gives a self-normalized epochwise radius of order $\kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } / ( \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } N _ { e } ^ { - } ) }$ up to logarithmic factors. Summing the resulting epochwise operator budgets over a geometric schedule gives

$$
\sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \alpha _ { h , k } \leqslant \widetilde { O } \bigg ( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) } \bigg )\tag{131}
$$

because geometric epochs still satisfy the same ${ \textstyle \sum _ { e } m _ { e } } / { \sqrt { N _ { e } ^ { - } } } \lesssim \sqrt { K }$ summability as the pure online sequence.

Applying Proposition 77, Theorem 10, and then Proposition 51 proves the executed-pair bound and finally transfers it to the target policy sequence with the $1 / ( \dot { 1 } - p )$ multiplicative factor and $p H K / ( 1 - p )$ additive exploiter cost. Substituting (130) and (131) yields (129). □

Corollary 79 (Warm-start-free epochwise linear theorem with burn-in). Assume the setting of Theorem $^ { 7 8 , }$ except that the explicit warm-start condition (89) is replaced by the following predictable excitation budget: for every stage $h \in [ H ]$ and epoch $e \in [ M ]$

$$
\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } \geqslant \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } N _ { e } ^ { - } ,\tag{132}
$$

where $\Omega _ { h , e } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } }$ is defined in (91) and $\underline { { \nu } } _ { h } > 0$ is stage dependent. Define

$$
N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ( \delta ) : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\lceil \frac { 6 4 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 4 } \log \left( 2 d _ { W , h } H M / \delta \right) } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ^ { 2 } } \right\rceil , \qquad \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { h \in [ H ] } \frac { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } } { 2 } .
$$

Let $c _ { \mathrm { e p } }$ be the epoch-schedule constant such that the cumulative number ofdeployed episodes before thefirst epoch with $N _ { e } ^ { - } \geqslant n$ is at most $c _ { \mathrm { e p } } n$ for every $n \geqslant 1$ . Then the target-policy regret satisfies the same bound as Theorem $^ { 7 8 , }$ , with $\nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ replaced by $\underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } ,$ plus the additive burn-in term

$$
c _ { \mathrm { e p } } H N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ( \delta ) .\tag{133}
$$

Proof. By (132) and Proposition 64, every epoch with $N _ { e } ^ { - } \geqslant N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ( \delta )$ satisfies

$$
\Lambda _ { W , h , e } ^ { - } \left( \lambda + \frac { \nu _ { h } N _ { e } ^ { - } } { 2 } \right) I _ { d _ { W , h } }
$$

simultaneously for all stages $h \in [ H ]$ , because the deviation term in (92) is then at most $\underline { { \nu } } _ { h } N _ { e } ^ { - } / 2$ Hence Corollary 75 recovers the same operator-transfer rate as Corollary 74, but with $\nu _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ replaced by $\underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ . From that point onward, the proof of Theorem 78 is unchanged.

Before the first warm epoch, the total number of deployed episodes is at most $c _ { \mathrm { e p } } N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ( \delta )$ by definition of $c _ { \mathrm { e p } } .$ . Each episode incurs regret at most H because the horizon is H and rewards lie in [0, 1]. Therefore the pre-warm contribution is bounded by (133), which yields the claimed warm-start-free result. □

Proposition 80 (Pure-online self-normalized linear calibration). Assume support control, Assumptions 14, 18, and 20. Consider the singleton-epoch schedule ${ \mathcal { E } } _ { k } = \{ k \}$ for $k \in [ K ]$ , so $M = K$ and $N _ { k } ^ { - } = k - 1$ . For every stage $h \in [ H ]$ and episode k $\geqslant 2 ,$ , define

$$
\Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } : = \lambda I _ { d _ { W , h } } + \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) \phi _ { W , h } ( X _ { h } ^ { j } ) ^ { \top }
$$

and

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) : = R _ { G , h } \sqrt { 2 d _ { W , h } \log \left( 1 + \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } ( k - 1 ) } { \lambda } \right) + 2 \log \left( \frac { 4 H K \mathcal { N } _ { F } ( \eta _ { F , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ) } { \delta } \right) } } \\ & { \qquad + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } + L _ { Y , h } + ( \sqrt { \lambda } + \kappa _ { W , h } ) L _ { \star , h } , } \end{array}
$$

where $\eta _ { F , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } : = ( ( k - 1 ) \vee 1 ) ^ { - 1 / 2 }$ . Assume the pure-online algorithm refits the ridge dual operator after every episode, defines the plug-in residual coordinates by

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ; x ) = \langle \widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ) , \phi _ { W , h } ( x ) \rangle ,
$$

and deploys only functions in the self-normalized confidence set

$$
\mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) } : = \left\{ f \in \mathcal { F } \colon \| \widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ) \| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) f o r a l l h \in [ H ] \right\} .\tag{134}
$$

Suppose furthermore that for every stage $h \in [ H ]$ and episode $k \geqslant 2 ,$

$$
\Omega _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } : = \lambda _ { \mathrm { m i n } } \left( \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { k - 1 } \underline { { Y } } _ { j } \right) \geqslant \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) ,\tag{135}
$$

where $\underline { { Y } } _ { j }$ is the predictable lower boundfrom Proposition 64 and $\underline { { \nu } } _ { h } > 0 .$ . Define

$$
N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\lceil \frac { 6 4 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 4 } \log \left( 4 d _ { W , h } H K / \delta \right) } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ^ { 2 } } \right\rceil , \qquad \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { h \in [ H ] } \frac { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } } { 2 } .\tag{136}
$$

Then with probability at least $1 - \delta ,$ simultaneously for all episodes k ⩾ $N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 2$ , the following hold.

(i) $f ^ { \dagger } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ , hence the optimistic choice over $\mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ satisfies Assumption 5.

(ii) For every stage $h \in [ H ]$

$$
\Big \| \widehat { \mathcal { T } } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } \Big \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \alpha _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) : = \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) } { \sqrt { \lambda + \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) / 2 } } .\tag{137}
$$

(iii) For every stage $h \in [ H ]$

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \left[ ( \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } \right] \leqslant \frac { 8 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \left( \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) \right) ^ { 2 } } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) } .\tag{138}
$$

Consequently,

$$
\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( K , \delta ) : = \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \sum _ { k = N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 2 } ^ { K } \frac { 8 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \big ( \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) \big ) ^ { 2 } } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) }\tag{139}
$$

is a valid exact residual budgetfor the post-burn pure-online deployed sequence.

Proof. Apply Proposition 73 with the singleton schedule $\mathcal { E } _ { k } = \{ k \}$ , so $M = K$ and $N _ { k } ^ { - } = k - 1$ using confidence level $\delta / 2$ . This yields, with probability at least $1 - \delta / 2 ,$ , simultaneously for all stages $h \in [ H ]$ , episodes k $\geqslant 2 .$ , and continuation functions $f _ { h + 1 } \in \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \left\| \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ) \right\| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) , } \end{array}\tag{140}
$$

where $\widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 }$ is the ridge refit on the prefix $\{ 1 , \ldots , k - 1 \}$ . Likewise, Proposition 64 with the same singleton schedule and confidence level $\delta / 2$ gives, with probability at least $1 - \delta / 2$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } \succeq \left( \lambda + \Omega _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { p r e d } } - 4 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \sqrt { ( k - 1 ) \log \left( 4 d _ { W , h } H K / \delta \right) } \right) I _ { d _ { W , h } } . } \end{array}
$$

On the intersection of these two events, if $k \geqslant N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 2$ , then (135) and (136) imply

$$
\Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } \succeq \left( \lambda + \frac { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) } { 2 } \right) I _ { d _ { W , h } } .\tag{141}
$$

We first prove $( i )$ . By Assumption 14, the reference function satisfies $\delta _ { h } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \equiv 0$ for every stage $h .$ Hence, by the operator-gap representation (116),

$$
\widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ; x ) = - \Bigl ( \widehat { \gamma } _ { h , f ^ { \dagger } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } \Bigr ) ( x ) = - \Bigl \langle \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } ) , \phi _ { W , h } ( x ) \Bigr \rangle .
$$

Therefore the plug-in residual coordinate of $f ^ { \dagger }$ is exactly

$$
\widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) = - \Big ( \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { \dagger } ) \Big ) .
$$

Taking $\Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 }$ -norms and using (140) yields

$$
\| \widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { \dagger } ) \| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } \leqslant \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) ,
$$

so $f ^ { \dagger } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ by (134). Since the algorithm chooses $f ^ { ( k ) }$ optimistically over $\mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ , the same argument as in Corollary 27 implies Assumption 5.

For (ii), Corollary 75 specialized to the singleton schedule gives

$$
\Big \| \widehat { T } _ { h , f ^ { ( k ) } } ^ { \sigma , ( k ) } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } - \mathcal { T } _ { h } ^ { \sigma } f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } \Big \| _ { 1 , \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } \leqslant \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) } { \sqrt { \lambda + \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) / 2 } } ,
$$

which is exactly (137).

For (iii), let $\theta _ { h } ^ { k }$ denote the exact residual parameter of the deployed function $f ^ { ( k ) }$ , so by Assumption 18,

$$
\delta _ { h } ^ { k } ( x ) = \langle \theta _ { h } ^ { k } , \phi _ { W , h } ( x ) \rangle .
$$

Pointwise, $\delta _ { h } ^ { k } = \widehat { \delta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { ( k ) } ) + e _ { h } ^ { k }$ , and both terms are represented in the same witness feature map. Hence

$$
\theta _ { h } ^ { k } = \widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { ( k ) } ) + \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 } \big ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } \big ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } \big ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } \big ) .
$$

Because $f ^ { ( k ) } \in \mathfrak { F } _ { \mathrm { o n } } ^ { ( k - 1 ) }$ and (140) holds,

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \| \theta _ { h } ^ { k } \| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } \leqslant \| \widehat { \theta } _ { h , k } ( f ^ { ( k ) } ) \| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } + \left\| \widehat { \vartheta } _ { h , k - 1 } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) - \vartheta _ { h } ^ { \star } ( f _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( k ) } ) \right\| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } \leqslant 2 \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) . } \end{array}
$$

Using $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname* { s u p } _ { x } \| \phi _ { W , h } ( x ) \| _ { 2 } \leqslant \kappa _ { W , h } } \end{array}$ and (141),

$$
\begin{array} { r l r } {  { \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } [ ( \delta _ { h } ^ { k } ) ^ { 2 } ] = \mathbb { E } _ { \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } } [ \langle \theta _ { h } ^ { k } , \phi _ { W , h } \rangle ^ { 2 } ] \leqslant \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \| \theta _ { h } ^ { k } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } } } \\ & { } & { \leqslant \frac { \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } \| \theta _ { h } ^ { k } \| _ { \Lambda _ { W , h , k - 1 } } ^ { 2 } } { \lambda + \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) / 2 } \leqslant \frac { 4 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } ( \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) ) ^ { 2 } } { \lambda + \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) / 2 } } \\ & { } & { \leqslant \frac { 8 \kappa _ { W , h } ^ { 2 } ( \beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) ) ^ { 2 } } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { h } ( k - 1 ) } , } \end{array}
$$

which proves (138). Summing this bound over all h and $k \geqslant N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 2$ gives (139).

Theorem 81 (Formal pure-online self-normalized linear / finite-rank kernel theorem). Assume support control, stable stage-game extraction, and the pure-online self-normalized variant ofProposition 80. Suppose further that Assumptions $I 4 , \ I 8 ,$ and 20 hold, and that the predictable growth condition (135) is satisfied. Define

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ d _ { F , h } , d _ { G , h } , d _ { W , h } \right\} , } \\ & { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ \kappa _ { F , h } , \kappa _ { G , h } , \kappa _ { W , h } \right\} , } \\ & { R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } : = \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ R _ { F , h } , R _ { G , h } , R _ { W , h } \right\} , } \\ & { \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } : = \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ R _ { G , h } + \sqrt { \lambda } S _ { G , h } + L _ { Y , h } + ( \sqrt { \lambda } + \kappa _ { W , h } ) L _ { \star , h } \right\} . } \end{array}
$$

Then the target-policy regret sequence generated by the pure-online self-normalized algorithm satisfies

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant H \big ( N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 1 \big ) } \\ & { \qquad + \frac { 1 } { 1 - p } \bigg [ \widetilde O \bigg ( H \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } / \sqrt { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log \bigl ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \bigr ) } \bigg ) } \\ & { \qquad + K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + p H K \bigg ] . } \end{array}\tag{142}
$$

The same statement applies to finite-rank kernel classes after lifting them into their explicit finite feature coordinates and interpreting $d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } , R _ { \mathrm { l i n } }$ in those coordinates.

Proof. Let $K _ { 0 } : = N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) + 1$ . The first $K _ { 0 }$ episodes contribute at most $H K _ { 0 }$ to regret because each episode has horizon H and rewards lie in [0, 1].

For the suffix $\{ K _ { 0 } + 1 , \ldots , K \}$ , Proposition 80 verifies approximate optimism and gives the exact residual budget $\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( K , \acute { \delta } )$ together with the operator radii $\alpha _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta )$ Since $\beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { { \scriptsize { o n } } } } ( \delta ) ~ =$ $\widetilde { \cal O } \big ( \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } k ) } \big )$ , (139) implies

$$
\Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( K , \delta ) \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } ^ { 2 } } { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \log \left( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \right) \right) ,
$$

where the extra logarithmic factor coming from $\textstyle \sum _ { k > K _ { 0 } } 1 / ( k - 1 )$ is absorbed by ${ \widetilde { O } } ( \cdot )$ . Therefore the residual contribution over the suffix is

$$
\sqrt { K H \Gamma _ { \mathrm { r e s } } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( K , \delta ) } \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log \left( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K \right) } \right) .\tag{143}
$$

Likewise, using (137) and the same bound on $\beta _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta )$ ,

$$
\begin{array} { r l } { \displaystyle \sum _ { k = K _ { 0 } + 1 } ^ { K } \displaystyle \sum _ { h = 1 } ^ { H } \alpha _ { h , k } ^ { \mathrm { o n } } ( \delta ) \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } } \displaystyle \sum _ { k = K _ { 0 } + 1 } ^ { K } \frac { 1 } { \sqrt { k - 1 } } \right) ~ } & { } \\ { \displaystyle \leqslant \widetilde { O } \left( H \frac { \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } \Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } } \sqrt { d _ { \mathrm { l i n } } K \log ( 2 + \kappa _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } R _ { \mathrm { l i n } } ^ { 2 } K ) } \right) . } \end{array}\tag{144}
$$

(145)

Applying the proof of Theorem 10 to the post-burn executed sequence, and then Proposition 51 to transfer back to the target-policy sequence, gives (142) after combining (143) and (145). The finite-rank kernel claim is immediate because an explicit finite-rank kernel lift is a finite-dimensional linear class in its feature coordinates, so the same argument applies verbatim after replacing the linear features by the lifted kernel features. □

Theorem 82 (Formal structured projected theorem under finite-dimensional lifts). Assume the setting of Theorem 54. Suppose in addition that for every stage $h \in [ H ]$ , the continuation class, the dual class, and the projected residual class admitfinite-dimensionalfeature lifts:

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } \subseteq \left\{ x \mapsto \langle \alpha , \varphi _ { F , h + 1 } ( x ) \rangle : \alpha \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { F , h } } , \| \alpha \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R _ { F , h } \right\} , } \\ & { \quad \mathcal { G } _ { h } \subseteq \left\{ x \mapsto \Gamma _ { h } ( x ) \vartheta : \vartheta \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { G , h } } , \| \vartheta \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R _ { G , h } \right\} , } \\ & { \quad \mathcal { P } _ { h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \subseteq \left\{ x \mapsto \langle \theta , \varphi _ { W , h } ( x ) \rangle : \theta \in \mathbb { R } ^ { d _ { W , h } } , \| \theta \| _ { 2 } \leqslant R _ { W , h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \right\} , } \end{array}
$$

with feature-norm bounds $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname* { s u p } _ { x } \| \varphi _ { F , h + 1 } ( x ) \| _ { 2 } \qquad \leqslant \qquad \kappa _ { F , h } , \operatorname* { s u p } _ { x } \| \Gamma _ { h } ( x ) \| _ { \mathrm { o p } } \qquad \leqslant } \end{array}$ $\kappa _ { G , h } , \operatorname* { s u p } _ { x } \| \varphi _ { W , h } ( x ) \| _ { 2 } \ \leqslant \ \kappa _ { W , h }$ , and projected residual envelopes $\begin{array} { r } { \operatorname* { s u p } _ { \psi \in \mathcal P _ { h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } } \| \psi \| _ { \infty } \leqslant B _ { W , h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } . } \end{array}$ Assume moreover that:

(i) the coefficient-space continuation model satisfies the affine coupling of Proposition $^ { 7 2 , }$ with derived constants $A _ { Y , h } ^ { \mathsf { P } }$ and $A _ { \star , h } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ;$

(ii) the explicit mixing rule (81) holds and the witnessfeature map satisfies the occupancy-averaged excitation condition (96) with constant $\hat { \omega } _ { h }$ ;

(iii) each $\mathsf { P } _ { h }$ is $L _ { 1 } ( \widetilde { \mu } _ { h } ^ { k } )$ -nonexpansive in the sense of (123);

(iv) the stagewise projection bias obeys (69);

(v) the projected operator gap satisfies the decomposition (124)–(125) with remainder level ϵ<sub>op,proj</sub>;

(vi) the projected backward-validation calibration assumption (70) holds for the epochwise / cross-fit sequence.

Define

$$
d _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } : = \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] } \{ d _ { F , h } , d _ { G , h } , d _ { W , h } \} ,
$$

Theorem 83 (Formal RKHS spectral-truncation projected theorem). Assume the setting of Theorem 54. For every stage $h \in [ H ]$ , let $\mathcal { H } _ { F , h + 1 } , \mathcal { H } _ { G , h } , \mathcal { H } _ { W , h }$ be separable RKHSs on the corresponding stagewise domains, with Mercer decompositions

and let $\Pi _ { F , h + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } , \Pi _ { G , h } ^ { ( m ) } , \Pi _ { W , h } ^ { ( m ) }$ be the corresponding orthogonal truncation operators onto the spans of thefirst m coordinates. Assume:

\begin{array} { r l } & { ( i ) ~ \mathcal { F } _ { h + 1 } \subseteq \{ f \in \mathcal { H } _ { F , h + 1 } : \| f \| _ { \mathcal { H } _ { F , h + 1 } } \leqslant R _ { F , h } \} , \mathcal { G } _ { h } \subseteq \{ g \in \mathcal { H } _ { G , h } : \| g \| _ { \mathcal { H } _ { G , h } } \leqslant R _ { G , h } \} , a n d } \\ & { \quad \mathcal { P } _ { h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \subseteq \{ \psi \in \mathcal { H } _ { W , h } : \| \psi \| _ { \mathcal { H } _ { W , h } } \leqslant R _ { W , h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \} ; } \end{array}
$$

(ii) the projected optimistic dual robust Bellman algorithm is run on the truncated classes

\mathscr { F } _ { h + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } : = \Pi _ { F , h + 1 } ^ { ( m ) } \mathscr { F } _ { h + 1 } , \qquad \mathscr { G } _ { h } ^ { ( m ) } : = \Pi _ { G , h } ^ { ( m ) } \mathscr { G } _ { h } , \qquad \mathscr { P } _ { h } ^ { \mathsf { P } , ( m ) } : = \Pi _ { W , h } ^ { ( m ) } \mathscr { P } _ { h } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ;
$$

Define

$$
\begin{array} { r l } &  \kappa _ { \mathrm { B A B S A } } ^ { ( m ) } : = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \vphantom { \operatorname* { m a x } ( \kappa _ { \mathrm { B A B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { C B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } \} } , \quad } \\ & { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B B D } } ^ { ( m ) } : = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \vphantom { \operatorname* { m a x } ( \kappa _ { \mathrm { B A B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } \} } } { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B } } ^ { ( m ) } } , \quad } \\ & { \beta _ { \mathrm { V B A B } } ^ { ( m ) } : = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \vphantom { \operatorname* { m a x } ( \kappa _ { \mathrm { A B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } \} } } { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } } , \quad } \\ & { \psi _ { \mathrm { d i m } } ^ { ( p ) } : = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \vphantom { \operatorname* { m a x } ( \kappa _ { \mathrm { B A B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } ) } } { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B } } ^ { ( m ) } \} } { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B } } ^ { ( m ) } } , \quad } \\ &  \psi _ { \mathrm { d i m } } ^ { ( p ) } : = \frac { \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \vphantom { \operatorname* { m a x } ( \kappa _ { \mathrm { B A B } } ^ { ( m ) } , \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C } } ^ { ( m ) } ) } } { \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B } } ^ { ( m ) } \} }  \kappa _ { \mathrm { B C B } } ^  \end{array}

Eigenvalue-decay RKHS projected consequence. Assume the setting of Theorem 83. Suppose further that there exist constants $C _ { F , h } , C _ { G , h } , C _ { W , h } > 0$ and exponents $r _ { F , h } , r _ { G , h } , r _ { W , h } > 0$ such that

r _ { \operatorname* { m i n } { } } : = \operatorname* { m i n } _ { h \in [ H ] } \left\{ r _ { F , h } , r _ { G , h } , r _ { W , h } \right\} .

$$

$$

$$
\Psi _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } ^ { \mathsf { P } , ( m ) } \leqslant \bar { \Psi } _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } ,\tag{150}
$$

$$
\underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { ( m ) } \geqslant \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } ,
$$

$$
B _ { W , \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { \mathsf { P } , ( m ) } \leqslant \bar { B } _ { W , \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { \mathsf { P } } ,\tag{151}
$$

$$
\epsilon _ { \mathrm { p r o j } } ( m ) + \epsilon _ { \mathrm { o p , p r o j } } ( m ) \leqslant C _ { \mathrm { t a i l } } \operatorname* { m a x } _ { h \in [ H ] }\tag{152}
$$

$$
\left( R _ { F , h } \sqrt { \sum _ { \ell > m } \tau _ { F , h , \ell } } + R _ { G , h } \sqrt { \sum _ { \ell > m } \tau _ { G , h , \ell } } \right.\tag{153}
$$

$$
+ R _ { W , h } ^ { \mathsf { P } } \sqrt { \sum _ { \ell > m } \tau _ { W , h , \ell } } \Bigg ) .\tag{154}
$$

Then for every m $\geqslant 1$

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \mathrm { R e g } ( K ) \leqslant H + c _ { \mathrm { e p } } H N _ { \mathrm { b u r n } } ^ { \mathrm { R K H S } } } \\ & { \qquad + \frac { 1 } { 1 - p } \widetilde { O } \left( \begin{array} { l } { H \left( \bar { B } _ { W , \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { \mathrm { P } } + \frac { \bar { \kappa } _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } \bar { \Psi } _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } } { \sqrt { \underline { { \nu } } _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } } } \right) \sqrt { m K } } \\ { \qquad \times \sqrt { \log \left( 2 + \bar { \kappa } _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { 2 } ( R _ { \mathrm { R K H S } } ^ { ( m ) } ) ^ { 2 } K \right) } } \end{array} \right) } \\ & { \qquad + \frac { K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { a p p r } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { e q } } + H K C _ { \mathrm { s p e c } } m ^ { - r _ { \mathrm { m i n } } } + H K \epsilon _ { \mathrm { d u a l } } + p H K } { 1 - p } , } \end{array}\tag{155}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { C _ { \mathrm { s p e c } } : = C _ { \mathrm { t a i l } } \underset { h \in [ H ] } { \operatorname* { m a x } } \left( R _ { F , h } \sqrt { \frac { C _ { F , h } } { 2 r _ { F , h } } } + R _ { G , h } \sqrt { \frac { C _ { G , h } } { 2 r _ { G , h } } } \right. } \\ & { \qquad \left. + R _ { W , h } ^ { \mathrm { P } } \sqrt { \frac { C _ { W , h } } { 2 r _ { W , h } } } \right) . } \end{array}
$$

By the integral test, for every $r > 0$ and $m \geqslant 1$

$$
\sum _ { \ell > m } \ell ^ { - 1 - 2 r } \leqslant \int _ { m } ^ { \infty } x ^ { - 1 - 2 r } d x = { \frac { m ^ { - 2 r } } { 2 r } } .
$$

Applying this to (149) gives

\sqrt { \sum _ { \ell > m } \tau _ { F , h , \ell } } \leqslant \sqrt { \frac { C _ { F , h } } { 2 r _ { F , h } } } m ^ { - r _ { F , h } } ,

Finite-rank kernel projected extension with burn-in. Assume the setting of Theorem $^ { 8 2 , }$ and suppose the finite-dimensional lifts there are induced by explicit finite-rank kernel feature maps of

ranks $m _ { F , h } , m _ { G , h } , m _ { W , h }$ . Let

Then

Proposition 84 (Compactly clipped ϕ-dual losses are bounded and Lipschitz). Fix a stage h and consider the scalar dual integrand

$$
\mathsf { I } _ { \phi , h } ( V , \eta , \nu ) : = \eta \sigma - \nu + \eta \phi ^ { \star } \left( \frac { \nu - V } { \eta } \right) , \qquad ( V , \eta , \nu ) \in [ 0 , H ] \times [ \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } , \overline { { \eta } } _ { h } ] \times [ - \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } , \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } ] ,
$$

with $0 < \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } \leqslant \overline { { \eta } } _ { h }$ . Define

$$
M _ { h } : = H + \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } , \qquad I _ { h } : = \left[ - \frac { M _ { h } } { \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } } , \frac { M _ { h } } { \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } } \right] .
$$

Assume $\phi ^ { \star }$ is continuously differentiable on $I _ { h } ,$ , and let

$$
B _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } : = \operatorname* { s u p } _ { u \in I _ { h } } | \phi ^ { \star } ( u ) | , \quad L _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } : = \operatorname* { s u p } _ { u \in I _ { h } } | ( \phi ^ { \star } ) ^ { \prime } ( u ) | , \quad M _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } : = \operatorname* { s u p } _ { u \in I _ { h } } | u ( \phi ^ { \star } ) ^ { \prime } ( u ) | .
$$

Then:

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { ( i ) \ f o r \ e \nu e r y \ ( V , \eta , \nu ) \in [ 0 , H ] \times [ \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } , \overline { { \eta } } _ { h } ] \times [ - \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } , \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } ] , } \\ & { \qquad \quad \vert \ l _ { \phi , h } ( V , \eta , \nu ) \vert \leqslant \overline { { \eta } } _ { h } \sigma + \overline { { \nu } } _ { h } + \overline { { \eta } } _ { h } B _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } = : B _ { \phi , h } ^ { \mathrm { c l i p } } . } \end{array}\tag{157}
$$

(ii) for any two triples $( V , \eta , \nu )$ and $( \widetilde V , \widetilde \eta , \widetilde \nu )$ in the same domain,

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \bigl | \mathrm { I } _ { \phi , h } ( V , \eta , \nu ) - \mathrm { I } _ { \phi , h } ( \widetilde { V } , \widetilde { \eta } , \widetilde { \nu } ) \bigr | \leqslant L _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } | V - \widetilde { V } | + \bigl ( \sigma + B _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } + M _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } \bigr ) | \eta - \widetilde { \eta } | } \\ & { \qquad + \bigl ( 1 + L _ { \phi ^ { \star } , h } \bigr ) | \nu - \widetilde { \nu } | . } \end{array}\tag{158}
$$

Consequently, any stagewise dual class whose $( \eta , \nu )$ outputs are clipped into those intervals satisfies the boundedness and pointwise Lipschitz requirements of Assumptions 10 and 11 with explicit geometry-dependent constants. In particular, the proposition applies to KL and $\chi ^ { 2 }$ ambiguity sets because their conjugates are continuously differentiable on every compact interval.

Proof. For every admissible $( V , \eta , \nu )$ , the quantity $z : = ( \nu - V ) / \eta$ satisfies $| z | \leqslant M _ { h } / \underline { { \eta } } _ { h } , \ : \mathrm { s o } \ : z \in I _ { h }$ This implies

$$
